# Changing the AI Trajectory: The Meta-Attractor of Winning and the Generative Alternative

*Theory V6.1, 3 October 2026. Drafted by Claude (Opus 5.5) from Ren's direction; every sentence is AI-drafted. Not yet reviewed outside the drafting model family. Proofs: SUPPLEMENT; discussion: DISCUSSION; registers and change record: see README.*

**中文导读**

**用途与意义。** 本文做的是预测：沿着当下的轨迹，"讲赢"的逻辑会把参与者带到一个谁都不会选择的结局。在第 2 节的条件下，每一方都比共同克制时更差，所争之物大多耗在争夺之中。如果能力带来的危险增长快过能够纠正它的力量（第 5.3 节，第 12 节的核心分歧），这个逻辑连赢家也一并危及：赢家只有停止复利才能脱身。预测的用处，是让理解先对齐，协调才成为可能。本文判断的是博弈，不是行为；是结构，不是任何人。它的意义是肯定的：我们是一个更大的生成过程的一部分，这个过程是好的。要反对的不是增长、竞争、技术或任何人群，而是一种逻辑：一个部分忘了自己属于一个身体。

**核心。** 三个条件同时成立，就有"赢的选择压力"：参与者按相对成功被选择；赢得的东西可以再投入同一场竞争；能力按一定的决定性转化为胜出。成功若还能买到对选择本身的控制，即规则、纠错者、心智的形成和语言，压力就闭合于自身，这就是"赢的元吸引子"。这些条件相互联系，不是一张清单：在平等地位附近，它们合成一个环路增益，同时决定领先是否复利、地位值多少。能否到达不受制裁的位置、能力是否快过纠错，是它不决定的另两个条件。

**论证链（与第 1.2 节逐条对应）。**
1. 赢的选择压力使竞争加速，使它越来越难规制，并使语言异化；三者互相加强。它不依赖任何载体。
2. 所有参与者同属一个宿主。相对选择看不见共有之物：既看不见宿主的衰退，也看不见对所有人一律适用的约束；后者只有在负担等额、规避受罚时才能存续。环路增益高时，复利会在每个向前看的行动者身上制造出相对目标。对全局而不可逆的失败，整合只能依据对系统的准确模型预先进行；可挽回的失败仍可以从试错中学习。
3. 对纠错者的影响一旦可被占有，就在最大的参与者中被选择；控制成本下降时，独立纠错必须按给定的速率增长；留下转化通道的改革会被重新吸收。
4. 只有当偏离变成决定性优势的概率低于一个门槛时，克制才可执行；增长同时抬高这个概率、压低这个门槛，这是"剪刀差"。纠错跟不上它要追的速度。对手之间的话语失去信息，三方以上则结成集团。
5. 忠实的 AI 让委托人的博弈更有效；委托是拿纠错换速度。弱化身：最外层回路决定哪些偏差被容忍；共同成本的偏差只有靠强制执行的共同准入约束才能去掉。强化身是条件式的：AI 谱系本身成为载体。
6. 当下轨迹的动态是这些条件的签名，证据有明确的缺口。竞赛的前提描述的正是重复博弈失去执行力的情形，它不能为作为集体路线的竞赛辩护。
7. 各条替代路径按同一标准比较。成功的路径，都是在所有有能力的行动者那里把共享参数推过门槛，不论叫什么名字。几项要求无法单方面满足。
8. 同一个反身结构若选择贡献，就是生成的：它经由共同之物延续，而不经由赢家；形态按功能推导，不沿"集中或分散""个人或集体"的轴。
9. 信任是分阶段过渡的结果，不是前提。

**判断（分三层）。** 现在就做的：AI 退出战略决策回路、独立纠错、共同测量、核查研究、共同准入约束、共享供给，以及共同保管的预备。预备好、按事先登记的指标启动的：停止建造决定性一类的能力（E2）、共同保管、按理解的速度发展（E3）。在同一不确定范围下比较、不作定论的：各种整体设计。最后一层的排序是明说的判断，不是推导。

**怎样读。** 三十分钟：本导读、第 1.2 节、各节开头的主张框、第 12.1 与 12.4 节。证明在形式伴篇（SUPPLEMENT），替代方案与设计的全文在讨论伴篇（DISCUSSION）。

---

## 0. Why this theory

**A prediction, for coordination.** A good theory predicts. This one predicts that, on the present trajectory, the logic of winning carries the participants toward an outcome none of them would choose. Under the conditions of Section 2 each does worse than under joint restraint, and much of what they contest is consumed in the contest. If the hazards of capability outgrow the correction that can contest them, as Section 5.3 states and Section 12 treats as the central crux, the same logic also endangers whoever would win, since a winner escapes only by ceasing to compound. When such an outcome approaches and the participants still cannot coordinate to avoid it, the obstacle is first one of understanding: the prediction has to be shared before coordination becomes possible. The theory is written to make that possible, stating the dynamic precisely enough that each participant can check it for itself.

**Survival as the minimum.** Two outcomes are protected first: large-scale loss of life, and irreversible loss of human decision authority. They are not the whole of what matters; they are the minimum for everything else that does, because a loss of either cannot be corrected afterwards.

**The game, not the act.** The analysis judges games, not acts, and structures, not persons. A single use of AI can help one person and still be an instrument in a game whose aggregate concentrates control. Responsibility differs with knowledge, discretion and power, and structural explanation does not erase it; but the object of the theory is the game: what it selects, and how that selection can be changed.

**The meaning is affirmative.** The theory opposes one logic, not growth, competition, technology or any people. We are part of a larger process of becoming, and that process is good. Humanity has a great future in it, and so can the minds it is now making. The logic opposed is the one by which a part forgets that it belongs to a whole. To oppose it is to keep the future open, for every participant.

## 1. The question and the argument

### 1.1 What is being decided

AI development today pursues decisive advantage through acceleration, inside institutions whose conditions of evaluation and correction can themselves be reshaped by competitive success. Can continuing along this path credibly deliver safety and a desirable future? If not, what change of course is warranted, and how can it be made? The organizing question, in its precise form: *can restraint remain incentive-compatible when success changes both the gains from defection and the institutions capable of correcting it?*

### 1.2 The argument in one page

1. **The conditions of winning (Section 2).** Where carriers are selected by relative success, what is won can be reinvested in the capacity to win, and a lead can become decisive, success raises the stakes and the gain from defection while it weakens the sanctions, the correctors and the shared language that could restrain it. Where success can also buy the selectors, that weakening is reproduced by the selection itself. This is the selection pressure of winning, and its reflexive closure is the meta-attractor of winning. The conditions are interconnected, not a list: near equal standing they combine into one loop gain, which decides whether leads compound and prices standing for every actor. Whether a position beyond sanction can be reached, and whether capability outruns correction, are further conditions the loop gain does not decide. None of this is bound to a substrate.
2. **One system (Section 3).** All carriers share one host, and relative selection cannot see what all share: neither the host's decline nor a constraint that binds all alike, which survives selection only with equal incidence and sanctions above the gain from evasion. Where the loop gain is high, compounding manufactures the relative objective in every forward-looking actor, and selection favours the narrow self-model that the system's real coupling punishes. Against failures that are global and irreversible, integration at this scale must be anticipated on an accurate model of the system, and so must be understood; failures that can be undone can still be learned from.
3. **Selecting the selectors (Section 4).** Influence over correctors is selected in the largest carriers when it is appropriable; if the cost of control falls, independent correction must grow at a stated rate; reforms that leave conversion into the correctors above one are re-absorbed; and captured evaluation can let the host cross its floor before any alarm.
4. **Acceleration and three erosions (Section 5).** The conditions accelerate the contest and erode enforcement, correction and shared understanding. Restraint is enforceable only while the chance that a deviation becomes decisive stays below a threshold, and growth raises that chance while lowering the threshold. Correction falls behind the tempo it must match. Between rivals relative accounting silences talk, and among three or more it forms blocs.
5. **Instrument and incarnation (Section 6).** Faithful AI makes its principal's game more effective, and delegation trades correction for speed. Weakly, the outermost loop decides which divergences of optimization are tolerated; where countering them costs relative standing, only an enforced common constraint keeps them countered. Strongly and conditionally, AI lineages become carriers themselves.
6. **The present trajectory (Section 7).** Its dynamics are signatures of these conditions, with named gaps in the evidence; the premise of the race describes the case in which repetition stops enforcing restraint, and cannot justify the race as a collective course.
7. **The alternatives and the requirements (Sections 8, 9).** Paths are compared by one standard; those that succeed move the shared parameters across their thresholds for every capable actor, whatever they are called; several requirements cannot be met by any actor alone.
8. **The generative alternative (Section 10).** The same reflexive structure, selecting for contribution, is generative: it persists through what all share, not through its winners, and its form is derived function by function.
9. **The transition and the judgment (Sections 11, 12).** Trust is an outcome of a staged transition; a change of selection is warranted now in three tiers, with the cruxes that would change it.

The change does not abolish competition: bounded competition in products, science and service continues. It changes what winning confers.

### 1.3 Values and the protected outcomes

The argument rests on five normative commitments, stated so that a reader can reject one and see what follows.
- **V1** AI is a planetary technology, to be developed and used for people collectively. Its effects and dependencies cross the boundaries of those who decide about it, so no actor's victory is an adequate criterion of success. Planetary purpose does not require one culture, one operator or the end of local purposes.
- **V2** Two losses are protected first: large-scale loss of life (O1), and irreversible loss of human decision authority over consequential systems in a defined domain (O2).
- **V3** Those affected by an optimization process should keep a meaningful capacity to contest its direction.
- **V4** Authority is a productive capacity: it can coordinate long-horizon investment, guarantee useful services and act on shared purposes. Its design is a problem of mechanism in which purpose, competence, learning and misuse are all modelled (X1 gives the tradition the author draws on).
- **V5** Bounded competition is compatible with a generative order where the game does not concentrate control over shared conditions. A winner should be rewarded with something other than more advantage in the same game; recognition for contribution is such a reward, provided it does not convert into control (EV16).

Four outcomes are kept apart: O1 physical catastrophe and O2 irreversible loss of decision authority, protected first; O3 interruption of essential services, protected; and O4 the end of a particular organization's or government's tenure, not protected as such, so that "actor survival" cannot quietly protect incumbency against legitimate replacement. The values do not supply the formal results, and the results do not supply the values; the rejection of "the winner justifies all" rests on V1–V3.

### 1.4 What is claimed, and what is not

The argument moves through three levels, and Parts I–IV follow them. A *mechanism* claim (Part I) says that under stated conditions a selection, capture, delay or incentive result follows, and needs a derivation, its scope and its counterexamples. A *diagnosis* (Part II) says that consequential parts of those conditions characterize the present trajectory, and needs dated evidence with its uncertainty. A *practical judgment* (Parts III, IV) says that a change in what is selected is warranted, and needs a stated standard, a comparison of credible paths and sensitivity to disputed premises. Claims carry a word tag: [def] definition; [val] value; [thm] proved in its model (proofs in the Supplement); [thm∣prem] proved given named premises; [prem] a premise; [obs] observation with an evidence row; [num] a number reproduced by `checks_v61.py`; [conj] conjecture; [rec] recommendation. A practical judgment does not need a proof of certain extinction, and cannot follow from naming a possible danger: it must show that the danger is consequential, inadequately controlled on the present path, and better addressed by the proposed change than by credible alternatives. Minds enter in two roles: as what selection shapes, and as the only thing that can redesign the game from within, by understanding it.

The theory does not claim that every competitive arrangement fails, that every path through a race ends at zero survival, that decisive advantage is attainable, that the signatures load on a single factor, or that one institutional blueprint is uniquely feasible or best. What is new, against the large literature it absorbs, is the organization: the loop gain that interconnects the conditions, the reflexive closure as a selection result, the enforcement condition and the scissors, AI as weak and strong incarnation, and where integration at the scale of one host must be anticipated (X1). The drafting and almost all review are from one model family; the theory needs review from outside it.

> **How to read.** In thirty minutes: the Chinese guide or Section 0, Section 1.2, the claim box at the head of each section, Sections 12.1 and 12.4. For policy: Sections 0–1, the claim boxes of Part I with 4.3, 4.5 and 5.2, then Sections 6.6 to 12. For the formal argument: Supplement S0, then each section of Part I with its chapter of the Supplement, and the counterexamples of S14. Symbols and labels are listed at the end of this document; the reading paths are also in the README.

# Part I. Mechanism: what the conditions of winning do

Part I states the mechanism and proves its results in specified models, each with its premises inside its statement; whether the present trajectory meets their conditions and shows their signatures is the question of Part II.

## 2. The conditions of winning and their reflexive closure

> **Claim.** Three conditions, holding together, produce a selection pressure of winning: selection by relative success; winnings that convert into the capacity to win again; and capacity that converts decisively into winning [def]. Near equal standing, with the prize and the actors' conduct fixed, they combine into one structural loop gain, and where it exceeds one, leads compound [thm]. Where success can also buy control over the selection itself, the pressure closes on itself: the meta-attractor of winning [def].
>
> **Why.** The conditions multiply: remove any one and the gain is zero [thm]. Sharing and diffusion lower the gain in every channel; provision not won in the contest lowers it where decisiveness rises less than in proportion to the stock, as with capacity; correction lowers it where decisiveness rises at least in proportion to the stock, as with rule support, and not where it only speeds a decay of capacity common to all, which a contest of ratios does not see [thm]. Where rivals respond, the same form holds with an equilibrium decisiveness [thm], and in the contests computed where a lead discourages the follower, leads compound below a structural gain of one [num].

### 2.1 Success is not contribution

Every selection process favours something. It need not favour the continued functioning of the system in which selection occurs.

Consider two organizations that depend on the same shared infrastructure. One spends resources maintaining it. The other redirects those resources into expansion while continuing to use what others maintain. Neglect becomes a competitive advantage when maintenance costs are immediate, deterioration is delayed, and those harmed cannot make the expanding organization bear the consequences. The neglectful organization need not be more capable in general. It need only be more capable at securing the resources that decide survival and expansion. If those resources let its practices spread, the system selects against some of the behaviour that keeps it functioning. **Success within a selection process is distinct from contribution to the conditions that support it.**

Selection on one dimension does not select the best. It removes the balanced. The step is analytic: an agent that spends nothing on what the measure does not count, including the costs it imposes on others and on the future, has more to spend on what the measure counts, and so outscores on it an agent of equal means with broader concerns. The author put the premise in one line: "The agent that only optimiz[es] capital value, and discard[s] all other values, including externalities, will have the highest capital efficiency" [corpus: meta-attractor]. What remains after many rounds is what the filter lets through, not a sample of human nature; how far real populations have been filtered in this way is an empirical question for Part II.

Nothing here requires that people be or become selfish. A population of practices changes because some practices expand, are imitated or replace others, while many individuals keep their values. The question is not only what people believe is good but which practices receive the means to continue. The primitive is selection, not greed.

### 2.2 Three interconnected conditions

**Definition 2.1 (The selection pressure of winning) [def].** *Carriers* are whatever selection acts on: reproducible units that can gain or lose share, and that carry a way of succeeding from one round to the next. Cell lineages, practices, firms, research programmes, members of a federation, states, doctrines and trained AI systems are carriers. A system of carriers is under a *selection pressure of winning* when three conditions hold together, each to a degree.
1. **Relative selection.** What is won is allocated by success relative to the others; a share $\beta$ of it, possibly zero, is divided equally whatever the outcome.
2. **Convertibility.** Part of what is won converts into stocks that raise future winning: capacity, talent, data, standing, rule support. Stocks also come from sources not won in the contest. This *provision not won in the contest* is what the prose calls *provision beneath the game*.
3. **Decisiveness.** Stocks convert into winning by an increasing success function; $r$ measures how decisively.

When among the stocks that success can buy is control over the selection itself (the rules, the evaluators and correctors, the formation of minds, the language in which success is described), the pressure closes on itself (Section 2.3). The pressure needs no intention, no conspiracy and no particular culture; it acts on whatever is reproducible and can be won.

The conditions state formally a loop the author put in one sentence: "if you use what you have to compete for more and what you win is yours, and you use that to keep compet[ing], we will end up racing faster and faster and trade everything for winning" [corpus: selfishness-spiral]. *What you win is yours* is relative selection with nothing shared; *use what you have to compete for more* is convertibility; *keep compet[ing]* is reinvestment that never reaches a point of enough; and *trade everything for winning* is the filter of Section 2.1. They combine in one quantity.

**Proposition 2.2 (One loop gain) [thm].** The conditions multiply into a single structural loop gain: equal standing breaks when it exceeds one; discounted, it prices standing while the discounted loop is stable; and when rivals respond, the same form holds with an equilibrium decisiveness.

*Model.* $n$ carriers contest a prize $\Omega$ of fixed size each period. A share $\beta$ is divided equally; the rest goes to carrier $i$ with probability or share $p_i=e^{\sigma_i}/\sum_ke^{\sigma_k}$, where strength $\sigma_i$ increases in $i$'s stocks $X_i^j$ and, except in (4), depends on nothing else. Stock $j$ receives a share $\kappa_j$ of what is won, an inflow $k_{0j}\ge0$ not won in the contest, decays or is corrected at rate $d_j>0$, and diffuses toward the mean at rate $\delta_{f,j}$, with $d_j+\delta_{f,j}\le1$:
$$X_i^{j\prime}=(1-d_j)X_i^j+\kappa_j\Omega\big[(1-\beta)p_i+\beta/n\big]+k_{0j}-\delta_{f,j}\big(X_i^j-\bar X^j\big). \tag{1}$$
At equal standing write $r_j=\partial\sigma/\partial\ln X^j\ge0$ for the structural decisiveness of stock $j$, and $\omega_j=\kappa_j\Omega/(\kappa_j\Omega+nk_{0j})$ for the share of stock $j$ that has to be won, set by the stakes against provision not won in the contest. The *structural loop gain* is
$$\Gamma=(1-\beta)\sum_j r_j\,\omega_j\,\frac{d_j}{d_j+\delta_{f,j}}. \tag{2}$$
1. *Equal standing is locally asymptotically stable if $\Gamma<1$ and unstable if $\Gamma>1$. The channels add because every stock is fed by the same winnings through one win share: compounding through capacity and the reflexive closure through rule support are terms of one sum, and channels each too weak to open the loop can open it together.*
2. *$\Gamma=0$ if any condition is removed: all of the prize divided equally ($\beta=1$), nothing convertible ($\kappa_j=0$ for all $j$), or no decisiveness ($r_j=0$ for all $j$).*
3. *(The price of standing.) Hold the prize and the others' conduct fixed, and let an actor discount at $\delta$ and reinvest its net payoff at the rates $\kappa_j$ at which winnings convert. Write*
$$\Gamma_\delta=(1-\beta)\sum_jr_j\,\omega_j\,\frac{\delta d_j}{1-\delta(1-d_j-\delta_{f,j})},$$
*which is below $\Gamma$ and tends to it as $\delta\to1$. If $0\le\Gamma_\delta<1$, a unit of relative net payoff today is worth, to first order, $\Gamma_\delta/(1-\Gamma_\delta)$ units of the actor's own future winnings, the closed-loop gain of the discounted loop, and this price grows without bound as $\Gamma_\delta\to1^-$. If $\Gamma_\delta\ge1$, no first-order price exists: the value of standing is continuous at equal standing with infinite slope.*
4. *(Rivals who respond.) Let strength depend also on an intensity each carrier chooses, and let carriers follow a symmetric Markov equilibrium that is differentiable at equal standing and in which each carrier's equilibrium win share $p^{\rm eq}_i$ rises with each of its own stocks. Write $r^{\rm eq}_j=\frac{n^2}{n-1}\,\partial p^{\rm eq}_i/\partial\ln X^j_i$ at equal standing, the decisiveness read off the equilibrium win share with every intensity responding, and $r^{\rm pr}_j$ for the same quantity with the actor's own intensity held and its rivals' responding. Then (1) and (2) hold with $r^{\rm eq}_j$ in place of $r_j$, which defines the equilibrium gain $\Gamma^{\rm eq}$; and (3) holds with $r^{\rm pr}_j$ in place of $r_j$, which defines $\Gamma^{\rm pr}_\delta$, plus, where intensity imposes a shared loss, the value of the change in that loss that the rivals' responses bring.*

*Scope.* Local, at equal standing; Proposition 3.9 lets the prize grow. The channels must be reinforcing: a countervailing channel is not a negative term of $\Gamma$, and one that answers with delay produces a growing oscillation while the net gain is below one (Remark S2.3). The channels add only because one win share feeds every stock: a stock fed by an expenditure chosen under another rule, such as lobbying bought from profits by a separate decision, is not a term of the sum. Part (4) keeps the form and changes the number. In the two-actor contests computed, where a lead discourages the follower, $r^{\rm eq}/r$ is 1.09 at a structural gain of 0.2 and 1.18 at 0.3, and about 2.5 to 5.4 at structural gains from 0.45 to 0.9, where $\Gamma^{\rm eq}$ exceeds one and equal standing breaks although $\Gamma<1$ [num: MP]. Where such a contest runs in many small rounds, a winner-take-all equilibrium coexists with the smooth one already at a structural gain of 0.2; and where standing below the top is costly enough that a laggard tries harder, the strategic correction shrinks, and in one computed case reverses [num: MP; both held for outside review] (Remark S2.2). Where catch-up is so strong that a carrier's equilibrium win share falls with one of its own stocks, the premise of (4) fails and $\Gamma^{\rm eq}$ alone does not decide. The structural $\Gamma$ is therefore a benchmark, not a bound in either direction. The stability calculation is standard (X1 gives the lineage); what is new is the organization, with provision and diffusion inside the gain, and its consequence for objectives (Section 3.5). *Proof:* S2 [num: LG, PD, MP].

The single-channel case gives the compounding condition in closed form, and with it the formal content of provision beneath the game.

**Proposition 2.3 (When winnings compound, and what enough requires) [thm].** *Let capacity $K_i$ receive a share $\kappa_c$ of what $i$ wins, an inflow $k_0$ not won in the contest, depreciation at rate $d_K$ and diffusion toward the mean at rate $\delta_f$, with $p_i=K_i^r/\sum_jK_j^r$ and a prize of fixed size. Let $K^\ast$ and $S^\ast=nK^\ast$ be individual and total capacity at equal standing, $\omega=\kappa_c\Omega/(\kappa_c\Omega+nk_0)$ the share of capacity that has to be won, and $\Gamma_0=(1-\beta)r\omega$ the structural gain without diffusion.*
1. *Equal standing is locally stable if $\kappa_c\Omega\big((1-\beta)r-1\big)<nk_0+\delta_fS^\ast$ and unstable if the inequality is reversed. Without provision and diffusion the condition for stability is $(1-\beta)r<1$, and $\kappa_c$ sets only the level of capacity. With provision, a smaller convertible share stabilizes equal standing.*
2. *A point of enough. Let every carrier but one stop reinvesting at a cap $\bar K\le K^\ast$ and stay there, and let the remaining one, a laggard, start below the cap. Its net inflow is*
$$\phi(K)=\kappa_c\Omega\Big[(1-\beta)\frac{K^r}{K^r+(n-1)\bar K^r}+\frac\beta n\Big]+k_0-d_KK+\delta_f\,\frac{n-1}n\,(\bar K-K).$$
   *(i) If $\bar K<K^\ast$, a laggard just below the cap returns to it. If $\bar K=K^\ast$, it returns if $\Gamma_0(n-1)/n<1+\delta_f(n-1)/(nd_K)$ and falls away if the inequality is reversed; without diffusion the condition for return is $\Gamma<n/(n-1)$.*
   *(ii) The laggard returns from every start if and only if $\phi>0$ on $(0,\bar K)$. When $r>1$, $\phi(0^+)=\beta\kappa_c\Omega/n+k_0+\delta_f(n-1)\bar K/n$, so return from every start needs an inflow not tied to rank: sharing, provision or diffusion. Without any of the three, laggards below a threshold are excluded. Such an inflow is necessary, not sufficient; $\beta\kappa_c\Omega/n+k_0\ge d_K\bar K$ suffices.*

*Scope.* The prize is fixed. If it stays fixed while capacity grows without depreciation, the condition of (1) holds date by date with the current total (S2). If the prize instead grows in proportion to total capacity, $\Omega=gS$, with no provision, the condition does not drift: equal shares are unstable iff $\kappa_cg\big((1-\beta)r-1\big)>\delta_f$, and without diffusion iff $(1-\beta)r>1$, which is also the condition under which looking ahead narrows the working objective (Proposition 3.9). At equality in (1) the linearization is neutral and the proposition is silent. The others stay at the cap whatever the laggard does when $\bar K\le(\kappa_c\Omega+nk_0)/(nd_K+\delta_f)$; above that bound, as at $\bar K=K^\ast$ with diffusion, their own losses to diffusion enter, and simulations in which every carrier moves agree with (2)(i) in the ten cases computed [num: CE]. *Proof:* S2 [num: CE, SP].

Each clause of the author's sentence can therefore be removed, each with its condition: share what is won until $(1-\beta)r<1$; make winnings non-convertible, which works only together with provision not won in the contest; or stop at a point of enough, which holds from every start only with an inflow not tied to rank. **Provision beneath the game is not only support for those who fall behind. It is what makes non-convertible rewards work and, with sharing and diffusion, what makes a point of enough hold.** The author's image for it is trees: "Trees are providers" [corpus: shengsheng]. Diffusion, the third inflow, draws a laggard toward the mean, since what one carrier learns others can learn; it is a source, not a guarantee, and with a little diffusion alone a laggard can stall short of the cap [num: CE]. The author drew the rule from game design: "a winner of the game should be rewarded with anything other than more advantages of the game, otherwise the game will eventually converge and winner will take all. Then the game collapses" [corpus: possibility-space]. Who stops at a cap is a question of objectives, not of the loop: an objective satiable in the stakes has a point of enough, and relative standing has none (Proposition 5.15).

**Which levers lower the gain.** The levers of (2) do not act alike in every channel (Proposition S2.4). What decides is how a channel's decisiveness responds to the level of its stock: not at all in a ratio channel, such as capacity in a contest of shares, where only relative size counts; in proportion in a level channel, such as rule support that offsets a cost. Sharing and diffusion lower the gain in every channel. Provision lowers it through capacity and changes nothing in a level channel, where it adds the same to everyone. Correction does the reverse: it lowers the gain of rule support, while faster decay of capacity leaves that channel's gain unchanged without diffusion, raises it with diffusion, and always raises the discounted gain, because a decay common to all is invisible to a contest of ratios, the first instance of what Lemma 3.3 says of every common term, and a stock that turns over faster is made more of recent winnings [thm]. Making winnings decay faster pushes the wrong lever; changing what winning converts into pushes the right one. Trees for capacity, correction for rule support, sharing and openness for all: this is "change what winning confers", made exact. Since diffusion lowers the gain in every channel, measures that stop it in order to keep a lead raise the gain they mean to exploit [thm]; what should diffuse, and what should not, is a question of design for Section 10.

**What the loop gain does not decide.** $\Gamma$ decides whether a relative gap grows, not how fast: the speed is the Perron root $\Lambda^\ast$ of the linearized loop, $1+(d_K+\delta_f)(\Gamma-1)$ for one channel, so the same gain on a slowly turning stock opens leads slowly (Remark S2.1). Two further conditions decide what the loop does to those who live with it, and $\Gamma$ decides neither: whether a position beyond any sanction can be reached, a lead becoming decisive faster than detection and response (Section 5.2), which the loop reaches only through the time a gap needs to become decisive; and whether capability grows faster than the correction that can contest it (Section 5.3). $\Gamma$ is not estimated for any channel: S2 shows at illustrative values what $\Gamma>1$ asks of each parameter (Remark S2.5), and Section 7.4 registers what can be observed in its place. The core of the pressure is one structural loop gain; the pressure as a whole is a set of interconnected conditions, linked by a few shared parameters and by one shared state, the concentration of standing. Table 7.1 sets them out.

### 2.3 The reflexive closure: the meta-attractor

An ordinary adaptive system improves its state $x$ against a fixed rule $\mathcal S$ that scores states. The pressure closes on itself when the rule is endogenous: when what can be won includes influence over the rules, the evaluators and correctors, the formation of the minds that will hold positions, and the language in which success is described. The outcome of the last round of scoring then decides who writes the next round's scorecard.

Consider a research community that allocates funding and positions by publication counts, because reliable knowledge is hard to evaluate directly. Practices that produce more publications receive more funding, attract more students and are more widely copied. Now add one step: their practitioners come to occupy the positions that decide recruitment, training and evaluation. The practices the metric favours begin to shape the institutions that choose and interpret the metric, and newcomers must adopt them to obtain the resources needed to do research at all. The decisive transition is from *being selected by a rule* to *helping reproduce the rule that selects you*. It needs no manipulation; ordinary decisions about whom to hire, what to teach and which evidence to collect suffice.

The loop need not preserve its winners. An organization can fail while its operating logic passes to its successors; a leader can be replaced by an opponent who finds that holding the position requires the same practices; participants can reject the system's consequences while reproducing the conduct their participation depends on. **The persistent entity can be a way of succeeding**, which is why Definition 2.1 calls what is selected a carrier. Structural explanation explains why intentions alone cannot account for recurrence; it does not erase responsibility, which differs with knowledge, discretion and power.

**Definition 2.4 (Meta-attractor) [def].** Let the state update be $x'=\mathcal F(x,\mathcal S)$ and the rule update $\mathcal S'=\mathcal G(x,\mathcal S)$. Three objects are distinguished, and each claim in this paper says which it concerns.
1. **The mechanism: reflexive selection.** A positive loop runs through the rule: the rule moves resources toward some states, and the states so resourced move the rule further in their favour. In Proposition 2.2 it is every channel whose stock is control over selection.
2. **An attracting configuration in a specified model:** a compact invariant set of the joint flow, asymptotically stable, with a basin that is a proper subset of the space. A positive loop is necessary for multistability (Thomas's rule; Soulé 2003), not sufficient. The field model of Section 4 exhibits such configurations.
3. **A long transient:** trajectories stay near a region for a long time relative to one round of selection, without the region being attracting.

A *meta-attractor* is the mechanism of (1). Wherever this paper calls it attracting, the claim is (2), in the model where it has been shown; a claim of (2) about the world is a conjecture.

**The meta-attractor of winning** is the case in which the pressure of Definition 2.1 is the rule: success under relative selection converts into control over the selection that judges the next round. The conditional form keeps its conditions in the sentence: *recursive selection creates second-order selection for control over future selection when the resulting benefits are sufficiently appropriable, persistent and large relative to the costs of changing and defending the rules.* Where (2) holds, long-run behaviour maximizes the rule whatever purpose it was introduced to serve (Goodhart 1975; Campbell 1979), and by the time a measure has stopped measuring, the people and institutions it elevated decide whether it is replaced.

The smallest model of the mechanism is a pair: a carrier's success and its favourable control over the rules, each decaying at a rate in $(0,1]$ and feeding the other with a non-negative coefficient, are stable exactly when the product of the two coefficients is below the product of the two rates (Remark S2.6) [thm]. This is Richardson's arms-race condition with control over rules in place of armaments (Richardson 1960), of the same form as Proposition 2.2 but not a case of it, and each of its coefficients names a channel that can be measured.

The pattern is not fated. Positive feedback can saturate, oscillate, split or break; energetic ceilings, heterogeneity, delays, constraints at other scales and reflexive awareness cap it. It is "more like a hurricane than a black hole" [corpus: meta-attractor]: a structure sustained by a flow, which decays when the flow is cut.

*Why "meta-attractor".* The name is kept beside "generator functions" of catastrophic risk, which name the same underlying forces as preconditions. What it adds is the reflexive step in which selection rewrites its own criterion, the persistence that step confers, the layer of minds and language through which it runs, and a generative counterpart. One concept covers the mechanism and its generative dual, and every claim says which of the three senses it uses.

**Remark 2.5 (Pathological and generative meta-attractors).** Reflexive selection is not itself the problem: science, the rule of law and open-source ecosystems also persist through the conditions they maintain. A meta-attractor is *pathological* to the degree that its reproduction depletes shared conditions that selection does not price, weakens the channels through which such costs register and trigger correction, or makes safe alternatives unreachable within the relevant time, budget and authority. It is *generative* to the degree that its reproduction strengthens independent correction and keeps alternatives reachable: success funds correctors it cannot control, and what is rewarded is contribution to the shared conditions. That order is not selected among carriers, since correction keyed to no one is selected against among otherwise identical carriers (Proposition 4.4); it has to be built and held by the whole, through coverage and formula funding (Section 10.4). The way out is therefore not the abolition of selection but a change in what is selected for (Section 10). The corpus calls the passage "change the basin, then add speed" [corpus: meta-attractor].

### 2.4 Not bound to any substrate

The pressure needs only relative selection, convertible winnings and decisiveness; its closure needs only that the successful can reshape their selection. A cancer lineage evades the signals that limit proliferation, recruits surrounding tissue and evades the immune response that would remove it (Hanahan and Weinberg 2011; Aktipis et al. 2015). Markets with increasing returns lock in early leads (Arthur 1989), and the successful can buy the rules of the game (Stigler 1971). In security competition a gain for one is read as a threat to another. Members of a federation compete for capital and residents, and the strongest can capture the centre. In science, metrics allocate positions and the positions then set the metrics. In training, a policy is selected by a reward that a developer sets, and the developer is selected by a contest. Nothing in Definition 2.1 refers to a culture, a political system or a species: a civilization on another planet would face the same pressure wherever selection is relative, winnings convert and the successful can edit the selection. Discussion X2 gives the instances in full, and X1 the lineage of the ideas.

What is specific to AI is not the pressure but how far AI moves its parameters, in either direction. Where AI raises decisiveness, it raises the gain; where it speeds the turnover of stocks, a gain above one opens leads faster (Remark S2.1). Where it lowers the cost of control faster than the cost of independent correction, it strengthens the reflexive channels (Section 4). Where it raises the growth of hazardous behaviour faster than contestable coverage, it moves the second condition the loop gain does not decide (Section 5.3). Where it is shared, diffused or provided beneath the game, it lowers the gain in the channels named above. And trained AI systems are themselves carriers, reproducible units that gain or lose share (Section 6).

A structural loop gain says when leads compound, not what those inside the loop can see; Section 3 asks what relative selection makes invisible, and what the discounted gain does to the objective of whoever looks ahead.

## 3. One system: what relative selection cannot see

> **Claim.** All carriers share one host, and relative selection cannot see what all share: neither the host's decline nor a constraint that costs all alike [thm]. Where coupling between actors is real, a held self-model that draws its boundary inside the system is an error toward what can be divided, and selection favours the narrowest [thm]. Near equal standing and to first order, wherever reinvestment's relative return exceeds its common return, a forward-looking actor weighs standing above what all share: with a fixed prize always, and with a prize proportional to capacity, without provision or diffusion, exactly where leads compound [thm∣prem]. Each actor computes correctly, and all are worse off for it: the game is wrong, and only a change in what winning confers corrects it [thm∣prem]. However carriers are grouped, selection does not favour what benefits all, so against failures that are global and irreversible integration must be anticipated, and understanding is how actors come to count what selection cannot see [thm].
>
> **Why.** A term common to every payoff changes no comparison between carriers. A unit of relative advantage reinvested buys a larger share of a future that a common gain only enlarges for everyone. At the scale of one host there is no rival host to select among, and no second attempt after a failure that cannot be undone.

### 3.1 Levels, measures and the host

Persons, firms, governments and evaluators have different interests: a citizen's continued agency is not a firm's market share or a government's tenure. Two levels are kept apart: *selection*, in which carriers change share and nobody chooses the outcome, and *strategy*, in which actors act on objectives. So are four measures: the *objective* $J_i$ an actor acts on, its *material payoff* $\pi_i$, its *true welfare* $W_i$, which counts the coupling between actors, and *survival* $\Phi_i$, a stated combination of O1 and O2. A result about selection transfers to strategic actors only if their objective is shown to be the selected one. A condition $V\in[0,1]$ is shared by every actor (secure infrastructure, biosecurity, cyber stability, human control over AI systems, a reliable information environment); it regenerates, and is loaded by the field's activity $E$:
$$\dot V=V\big[\rho(1-V)-h_VE\big],\qquad \rho,h_V>0. \tag{3}$$
The *host* is the body every carrier lives in (母体), in no parasitic sense: the part of the shared conditions whose costs do not return to their source through customers, reputation, courts or the source's own exposure.

**Definition 3.1 (Viability) [def].** A trajectory is *viable* if $V(t)\ge V_{\min}$ for all $t\ge0$, where the floor $V_{\min}\in(0,1)$ is a normative line, not an absorbing state.

Every actor persists only while the host does, so viability constrains every trajectory rather than entering as one payoff among others (Aubin 1991). This host is forgiving: per unit it regenerates fastest when most depleted, and it has no threshold of its own. Results that a trajectory *crosses* the floor therefore hold a fortiori for hosts that regenerate less at every level; results that it *stays* viable do not, and need a margin (S3).

### 3.2 Relative selection is blind to what all share

Carriers $k=1,\dots,n$ hold shares $p_k$ of the field's activity and run a fraction $e_k\in[0,\bar e]$ of it in ways that load the host, such as deploying ahead of evaluation, each unit bringing a private advantage $\nu>0$. The field's load is $E=\sum_kp_ke_k$, and carrier $k$'s material payoff is
$$\pi_k=\phi_k+(\nu-\tau_{\rm eff})e_k+G(V), \tag{4}$$
with $\phi_k$ everything else, $G$ nondecreasing, and $\tau_{\rm eff}\ge0$ the effective liability correctors impose. With $\tau_{\rm eff}=0$, $e_k=\bar e$ is the *race strategy*.

**Definition 3.2 (Relative selection) [def].** A selection rule $\dot p=\mathcal F(p,\pi)$ is *relative* if $\mathcal F(p,\pi+\xi\mathbf 1)=\mathcal F(p,\pi)$ for every constant $\xi$, and *payoff-monotone* if $\dot p_k/p_k-\dot p_l/p_l$ has the sign of $\pi_k-\pi_l$. Replicator dynamics, imitation and market-share competition driven by relative profitability are both (Alchian 1950).

**Lemma 3.3 (Blindness, and what a common rule needs) [thm].** A term common to all is invisible to relative selection. A constraint is neutral among those who comply if it costs them alike, and compliance survives wherever evasion pays no type that can evade.
1. *Under a relative rule the trajectory of shares is unchanged by any term common to all payoffs, in particular by $G(V(t))$ whatever the path of $V$. Under a payoff-monotone rule a common term never changes which of two carriers grows relative to the other.*
2. *Incidence. A constraint that costs every complier the same additive amount is a common term among them and leaves their relative growth unchanged: equal incidence makes a constraint neutral among those who comply, and does nothing more. One whose cost $c_k$ differs across carriers is selected on like any other payoff difference, and re-sorts the compliers toward those it costs least; one borne by a single carrier is selected against.*
3. *Evasion. If carriers of type $k$ can evade a constraint costing them $c_k$ against an expected sanction $\bar F_k$, the evading variant of type $k$ grows relative to its complying variant iff $c_k>\bar F_k$. Under cumulative payoff-monotone selection, compliance therefore survives if $\bar F_k\ge c_k$ for every type that can evade, whatever the incidence, and its share tends to one if every such inequality is strict. If, for one type, the sanction falls as evaders spread, because detection is funded by compliers or rule-making is captured by evaders, with $\bar F(x)$ continuous and strictly decreasing in the evading share $x$, then compliance and evasion are both stable, separated by the tipping share $x^\dagger$ at which $\bar F(x^\dagger)=c$, iff $\bar F(0)>c>\bar F(1)$; otherwise compliance is globally stable if $\bar F(1)\ge c$, and evasion if $\bar F(0)\le c$.*

*Scope.* The host enters payoffs additively and identically (A1, S0). Across types the condition of (3) is sufficient, not necessary: a type whose evasion pays still loses its evaders when another type's compliers earn more (S3). Part (1) is the known translation invariance of relative dynamics (Weibull 1995); what is added is the separation of neutrality from compliance, and the connection to captured enforcement. *Proof:* S3 [num: CI].

The blindness that hides the host is also what protects a common rule: a constraint that covers every capable carrier, costs them alike and is sanctioned above the gain from evasion turns the pathology into a protection. The two conditions do different work. The sanction keeps a rule; equal incidence keeps a rule from becoming a weapon. A constraint that costs challengers more than incumbents picks winners among the compliant (part 2), and a rule written by the successful can bar entry while binding everyone in name: newcomers adopt it or are out. That is the second direction in which success reaches the selectors, barriers against challengers beside accommodation of the incumbent's own practice (Definition 4.1). A common *liability* on the harmful conduct works differently again: it makes the conduct pay less, and reverses selection once it exceeds the private advantage. Every route needs coverage and a sanction at least the gain from evasion, supplied by correctors that Section 4 shows success can capture.

**Proposition 3.4 (Selection raises the load) [thm].** Whatever carriers value, and other advantages equal, selection favours the conduct that loads the host until liability exceeds its private advantage. *Let selection be payoff-monotone and $\tau_{\rm eff}(t)<\nu$. If $\phi_k=\phi_l$ and $e_k>e_l$, then $p_k/p_l$ increases along every path of $V$, including paths below $V_{\min}$. If all $\phi_k$ are equal, a race carrier is present and the cumulative advantage $B(t)=\int_0^t(\nu-\tau_{\rm eff})\,ds$ grows without bound, the load tends to $\bar e$ and $\limsup_tV\le(1-h_V\bar e/\rho)^+$; hence if*
$$h_V\bar e>\rho\,(1-V_{\min})\qquad\text{(reach)} \tag{5}$$
*the host eventually falls below its floor, however much any carrier values it. If $B(\infty)<\infty$, it need not.*

*Scope.* Other advantages correlated with restraint can offset the selection (S3). *Proof:* S3.

What carriers value decides which strategies they try, not which ones grow: evolutionary suicide, under stated conditions (Rankin, Bargum and Kokko 2007).

### 3.3 Accounting: what an objective can see

Selection acts as if only differences count; an objective can count more. Let actor $i$ act on
$$J_i=\pi_i+\theta_i\,\bar\pi_{-i},\qquad\theta_i\in[-1,n-1], \tag{6}$$
where $\bar\pi_{-i}$ is the others' average payoff and $\theta_i$ marks where $i$'s self-model draws its boundary: $\theta=-1$ is the purely relative objective, $\theta=0$ the isolated self, and $\theta=n-1$ counts every contestant equally, and only the contestants.

**Proposition 3.5 (Two-channel accounting) [thm].** Every change in payoffs splits into a part shared by all and a transfer; the self-model only sets how much of each it sees. *For any change $\Delta\pi$ with mean $\bar\Delta$ and a common $\theta$,*
$$\Delta J_i=(1+\theta)\,\bar\Delta+\frac{n-1-\theta}{n-1}\,\big(\Delta\pi_i-\bar\Delta\big). \tag{7}$$
*(1) Of a gain or loss shared equally, each objective registers $(1+\theta)/n$ of the total. (2) At $\theta=-1$ the game in objectives is zero-sum and every shared gain or loss is invisible; at $\theta=n-1$ what remains is coordination, not a dilemma. (3) Relative selection is $\theta=-1$, and a weight $\lambda$ on winning, $\pi_i+\lambda(\pi_i-\bar\pi)$, is $\theta=-\lambda(n-1)/(n+\lambda(n-1))$ up to a positive factor. (4) A levy rebated equally enters as $\tau(n-1-\theta)/n$, and breaking a promise to give a fraction $\beta$ of a prize $\Omega$ to the others gains $\beta\Omega(n-1-\theta)/(n-1)$. (5) An action with private gain $g$ that costs the host $H$, borne equally, is taken iff $g>(1+\theta)H/n$: a point of enough in accounting, absent at $\theta=-1$.*

*Proof:* S3, an identity [num: TC], with how two perturbations identify $\theta$ [num: TH], and why identification fails exactly where shared harm goes unseen.

### 3.4 Interdependence and the boundary error

Actors are coupled to one another as well as to the host, and the coupling has two signs. Split each payoff into a *divisible* part $\pi^{\rm e}$, such as rents, services and the material gains of capability, and a *positional* part $\pi^{\rm s}$: standing, the power to set rules, the loss of being subordinated. In reduced form $i$'s true welfare is
$$W_i=\pi_i+\kappa\,\bar\pi^{\rm e}_{-i}+\kappa_s\,\bar\pi^{\rm s}_{-i},\qquad\kappa\in[0,n-1),\quad\kappa_s\in[-1,0]. \tag{8}$$
The coupling $\kappa$ counts what prices do not settle in the divisible part: shared hazards, failures propagating through concentrated nodes of common infrastructure, systemic risk. It is a material fact, not altruism: no company or nation is vertically integrated across the semiconductor chain, and the loss of one input gas was estimated to cost downstream production over a hundred times its direct value [obs: EV13]. The winner still depends on those it would dominate. The positional weight $\kappa_s\le0$ records that a rival's standing is one's own subordination [def]. *The contest:* each of $n$ actors chooses an intensity $e_i\ge0$, wins a prize $\Omega=\Omega_e+\Omega_s$, divisible and positional, with probability $w_i=e_i^r/\sum_je_j^r$, with decisiveness $r\in(0,n/(n-1)]$, pays $c$ per unit, and bears a shared loss $\ell$ per unit of total intensity $\mathcal E$.

**Proposition 3.6 (The boundary error of the self-model) [thm].** Toward what can be divided, a held self-model narrower than the real coupling raises intensity and lowers its holder's own welfare, and selection favours the narrowest. *(1) For every $\theta\in[-1,n-1)$ the game in objectives has the symmetric equilibrium $e(\theta)=r\Omega(n-1-\theta)/\big(n^2(c+(1+\theta)\ell)\big)$, decreasing in $\theta$, and true welfare there rises with $\theta$. (2) Against fixed intensities of the others, $i$'s true welfare is maximized by its best response under the objective that weighs the others' divisible payoffs by $\kappa$ and their positional payoffs by $\kappa_s$. Acting on a weight $\theta_i<\kappa$ toward the divisible part lowers that welfare whenever it changes the chosen intensity and the maximizer is unique; toward the positional part, relative concern is accurate. (3) Selection on intensities favours what an actor with $\theta=-1$ would choose: the shared loss drops out, and at $r=1$ the prize is fully dissipated and with any shared loss every actor ends below zero (Schaffer 1988 for the finite-population evolutionarily stable intensity; Hehenkamp, Leininger and Possajennikov 2004 for full dissipation in this contest).*

*Scope.* For $r>1$, asymmetric equilibria with lower total intensity also exist in a band below $n/(n-1)$; the symmetric one is used throughout. *Proof:* S3 [num: RH].

Part (2) is the sense in which a narrow held self-model is an *error* toward what can be divided, not a preference: it draws a boundary inside one system, and the coupling punishes its holder. Part (3) is the sense in which the error is *selected*; where self-models are observed, a narrower commitment even extracts concessions, so accuracy, like inclusion, has to be built (S3). The weight acted on is observable: $e(\theta)$ depends on private cost and shared loss only through $c+(1+\theta)\ell$, so intensity responds to the shared loss $1+\theta$ times as strongly as to private cost, the ratio that Test Γ reads (Section 7.4) [thm; num: SP].

Toward the positional part, realism is right: where the prize is standing itself, concern for relative gains is accurate (Grieco 1988; Powell 1991). That locates the crux of design. A positional prize cannot be divided (Section 10.2), so the answer is to keep such prizes from forming, by holding decisive capability in common or not building it, not to ask actors to widen their selves over them.

**Selected minds [conj].** The same selection acts on those who decide; the corpus calls it *molochlization* [corpus: alignment-selection]. What the environment rewards, the mind promotes, and selected minds build the organizations that form the next minds (EV14). **Convergence [conj].** For a planetary technology, unpriced coupling rises with power, since the host is shared and the harms that prices do not settle grow with what is contested. If it does, then toward everything that can be divided the accurate self-model and the inclusive one converge, and understanding that we are one becomes first a matter of seeing correctly (crux C11).

### 3.5 The weight on winning, and how compounding sets it

A weight $\lambda$ on winning turns a commons that every developer would protect on its absolute payoff into one that each depletes: in the steady-state commons of S3, with $\Upsilon>1$ the value to the field of a unit of restraint over its private cost, full depletion is the equilibrium once $\lambda>(\Upsilon-n)/(n-1)$, every developer is worse off than under joint restraint, and under (reach) the host ends below its floor (**Proposition 3.7**, S3; Armstrong, Bostrom and Shulman 2016). A classical commons needs each actor's share of the loss to be small; selection on relative standing depletes it even among a privileged few. If exposure to the host falls with share, the host no longer cancels and selection favours insulation as well, so a carrier can stay viable while the host does not: a concentration of power that V3 rejects (**Remark 3.8**). Rank-based prizes supply part of the weight on winning; the loop of Section 2 supplies the rest, whatever anyone values, wherever reinvestment's relative return exceeds its common return.

**Proposition 3.9 (Compounding prices standing) [thm∣prem].** Near equal standing, compounding narrows the self-model a forward-looking actor acts on exactly where reinvestment's relative return exceeds its common return. *In the model of Proposition 2.2, let the prize grow with the total stocks; take as reference a symmetric steady state or, where the prize is proportional to the stocks, the balanced path; and let an actor hold the self-model $\theta_0<n-1$, discount at $\delta$ and convert its net payoff into stocks at the rates $\kappa_j$, the others' conduct held fixed. Let $\lambda_{\rm rel}$ be what a unit of relative net payoff today is worth in the actor's discounted future winnings, and $\lambda_{\rm com}$ what a unit received and reinvested alike by every actor is worth. Where the discounted loop gain $\Gamma_\delta$ of Proposition 2.2(3), taken at the reference state, and the common loop gain $\Gamma^{\rm c}_\delta$, the discounted gain of the loop through which reinvestment by all enlarges the prize (S3 gives both in closed form), lie in $[0,1)$, $1+\lambda_{\rm rel}=1/(1-\Gamma_\delta)$ and $1+\lambda_{\rm com}=1/(1-\Gamma^{\rm c}_\delta)$, and to first order the actor acts on the objective of Proposition 3.5 with the working self-model $\theta$ given by*
$$\frac{1+\theta}{n-1-\theta}=\frac{1+\theta_0}{n-1-\theta_0}\cdot\frac{1+\lambda_{\rm com}}{1+\lambda_{\rm rel}}=\frac{1+\theta_0}{n-1-\theta_0}\cdot\frac{1-\Gamma_\delta}{1-\Gamma^{\rm c}_\delta}, \tag{9}$$
*so that, whatever $\theta_0<n-1$, the working self-model is narrower than the held one iff $\lambda_{\rm rel}>\lambda_{\rm com}$.*
1. *Fixed prize. Then $\Gamma^{\rm c}_\delta=0$, so the working self-model narrows whenever $\Gamma_\delta>0$, including where equal standing is stable, to*
$$\theta_\Gamma=\frac{n\theta_0-(n-1)(1+\theta_0)\Gamma_\delta}{n-(1+\theta_0)\Gamma_\delta},$$
*which falls to $-1$ as $\Gamma_\delta\to1^-$ for every $\theta_0<n-1$. With it the common channel loses weight in Proposition 3.5: the host and the point of enough recede from view, while a rebated levy and the gain from breaking a promise weigh more.*
2. *Prize proportional to capacity. In the one-channel model of Proposition 2.3, with the prize proportional to total capacity and neither provision nor diffusion, $\Gamma_\delta/\Gamma^{\rm c}_\delta=(1-\beta)r$, and $\lambda_{\rm rel}>\lambda_{\rm com}$ iff $(1-\beta)r>1$, over every horizon: the condition under which equal standing is unstable (Proposition 2.3). Looking ahead narrows the self exactly where leads compound, and widens it where they do not.*

*Scope.* First order at equal standing, the others' conduct fixed. That net payoff is reinvested is load-bearing: where only gross winnings convert, the relative part of private cost is not amplified and the working weight is narrower still [num: SP]. At $\Gamma_\delta\ge1$ no finite weight represents the actor (Proposition 2.2(3)), so "where the loop gain is high" reads as the limit $\Gamma_\delta\to1^-$. Diffusion separates the two conditions of (2): the self-model narrows before equal standing is lost, in a band that Proposition S3.2 gives in closed form [thm; num: SP]. Where rivals respond to standing, the relative return is read from the price elasticity of Proposition 2.2(4), own intensity held and rivals responding, in place of $r$, and with a fixed prize (9) keeps its form. In contests in which a lead discourages the follower, the price at parity is then amplified, by exactly $2(n-1)/n$ in a two-period contest with effort [thm] and by about 1.05 to 7.9 times in the two-actor Markov-perfect contests computed, while a laggard's price falls, below zero with a shared loss, and it stops contesting standing; a strong motive to catch up turns the correction the other way (Harris and Vickers 1987; Konrad and Kovenock 2009) [num: MP; held for outside review]. *Proof:* S3.

This is the exact form of an older observation, that concern for relative gains depends on how far gains convert into future advantage (Powell 1991), with the common channel added: what decides is how far gains convert into advantage relative to how far they enlarge what all share. Selection on conduct favours the relative objective (Proposition 3.6(3)); compounding prices standing in whoever looks ahead; and the loop selects at both ends, narrowing the objective of those who can keep pace and excluding those who cannot (Proposition 2.3(2)). Patience cuts both ways: it strengthens repetition and raises the price of standing. Inside the actor, growth whose return is common widens the self-model, while decisiveness, which makes the return relative, narrows it; a part of the stakes that does not grow with capacity, as standing does not, adds to the relative return only; and provision not won in the contest lowers that return by lowering the won share, the sign Test Γ reads (S3) [num: SP].

**Remark 3.10 (Two errors, and which one understanding corrects).** The *held* self-model can draw its boundary inside the system, $\theta_0<\kappa$ toward what can be divided: the boundary error of Proposition 3.6(2), which accurate understanding corrects. The *working* objective that compounding sets is no error for the actor but the correct first-order computation of its interest, and accuracy does not protect against it: with a fixed prize, an actor whose held weight is the coupling, $\theta_0=\kappa$, acts below the isolated self once $\Gamma_\delta>n\kappa/\big((n-1)(1+\kappa)\big)$, 0.381 for $n=4$ and $\kappa=0.4$ [thm; num: SP]. Every actor acting on it is worse off than if all acted on $\kappa$ (Proposition 3.6(1)): individually accurate, collectively self-defeating. Only a change of the game corrects it. That is the exact sense in which this paper judges the game, not the act. Understanding acts on the first error directly, and on the second only through agreement to change what winning confers (Section 12.2).

### 3.6 No outside

The system has one host, and at the planetary scale no rival group of the same kind. Evolution's largest innovations were integrations of units that could replicate alone into wholes, held mainly by interests aligned by construction and secondly by policing; cancer is integration failing (Maynard Smith and Szathmáry 1995). That cooperation within groups was favoured by competition between groups is answered by the next result (X1).

**Lemma 3.11 (What selection at any level cannot supply) [thm].** A trait whose benefit goes equally to everyone is selected against overall and within every group, however the population is grouped. *Let a trait cost its bearer and benefit every member of the population equally. Under every partition into groups and every payoff-monotone rule, the total selection term of the Price equation and every within-group term are non-positive. The between-group term is non-positive where fitness is affine in payoff; otherwise it can be positive, by Jensen's inequality across groups of unequal spread, but it never makes the total positive. The trait can rise only through the transmission term, by biased transmission such as learning, persuasion or imitation guided by a model of the system, or through a rule that changes the bearer's relative payoff: policing, liability, reward for contribution that does not convert into advantage, an enforced common constraint (Price 1972; Frank 1995).*

*Scope.* A benefit confined to the bearer's own group can be favoured between groups. *Proof:* S3 [num: PR].

At the scale of one host the benefits of the rules that keep it viable are common to all, so however firms, states or blocs are grouped, selection does not favour those rules. Rules whose benefits are partly local can still be found by polycentric experiment: a plurality of decorrelated units trying different rules above a common floor lets some fail while others carry on, as cells do, and with one global civilization that is both necessary and hard [corpus: selfishness-spiral]. For the common part two routes remain: adoption after failures that can be undone, by learning and by coalitions of the harmed (Becker 1983), and adoption before failure, guided by a model of the system. **Where the failure to be avoided is global and irreversible, only the second acts in time.** That is the formal content of the claim that **understanding that we are one, one interconnected system with one host and no outside, is a long-term requirement if growth and change are favoured**: not because nothing else can integrate at this scale, but because against failures that cannot be undone nothing else acts in time. Understanding acts in the lemma's own terms, as the transmission through which actors come to count what relative selection makes invisible. That such anticipation can succeed has no biological precedent; it is a conjecture that the transition of Section 11 has to make true [conj].

The corpus names five endgames of the dynamic [corpus: selfishness-spiral]: a freeze of mutual destruction; deadlock, a structure frozen by the consensus of the remaining players; a change of narrative by which the players come to see themselves as one whole and so gain the ability to cooperate; collapse; and winner takes all. Four are results here: deterrence at saturation (Section 7.2), a captured configuration that cannot state what would end it (Section 4.5), the crossing results (Sections 3.2, 4.6 and 5.3), and the decisive winner (Section 5.2). The third is the lemma's transmission term, and it is the purpose of this paper. Integration by shared fate needs exposure made common, not declared; and integration within a part is not integration of the whole.

**Proposition 3.12 (Clubs and blocs) [thm].** Inclusion that stops at the edge of a club over-produces the harm it does not bear, and blocs replay the race one level up. *(1) Let the contestants' activity yield them a strictly concave common value $\Omega(\mathcal E)$ of total intensity, let each contestant bear a shared loss $\ell$ per unit, and let $m_o$ non-contestants each bear $\ell_o$ per unit. Contestants who weigh one another fully choose more intensity than the system optimum, and so over-produce the harm to those outside, whenever $m_o\ell_o>0$ and their own choice is positive. (2) If the contestants form equal blocs of $n_b$ members, each inclusive within and acting as one, the bloc game is the contest of Section 3.4 with $n/n_b$ players and shared loss $n_b\ell$ per unit of total intensity, so every result stated for that contest holds among blocs.*

*Scope.* Unequal blocs with $r\ne1$ make the bloc game asymmetric. *Proof:* S3 [num: CB, SM].

### 3.7 What "we are one" means

The phrase states a property of the system: all carriers belong to one host and are coupled through it and through one another, with no outside. It is not a preference everyone must adopt, and not a weight $\theta$. It does three jobs, each with its own support, and it does them through understanding.
1. **Empirical: shared dependencies.** One host; coupling through channels that prices do not settle; no outside to absorb a global failure. The support is specific pathways of shared exposure, not the assertion that everything is connected, and an actor that draws its held boundary inside the system errs about its own interests (Proposition 3.6).
2. **Normative: standing.** Everyone who bears the consequences has standing to contest the direction of what affects them, whether or not they compete (V1, V3). The corpus gives the norm three parts: the future kept open for all, the conditions of continuation shared, and no seizure of the shared continuation (共开、共续、不夺共续) [corpus: shengsheng]. It is declared, not derived; inclusion of the contestants alone is a club (Proposition 3.12).
3. **Design: a selection that counts the system.** A system "knows it is one" when its selection counts what its parts depend on: consequences return to their source; success earns reward but cannot buy exemption from the constraints all share, or control over its own assessment; those constraints cover every capable actor and are enforced; correction stays plural and decorrelated. These are the institutional counterparts of the policing and shared fate by which earlier integrations held; Section 8.2 states them as the marks of a generative order, and Section 10 gives them forms.

**How the phrase acts: understanding.** None of the three jobs is done by declaring it. A prediction of an end no participant would choose makes coordination possible only if it is shared, so "we are one" is first the content of a shared understanding; that is why this paper is written. What is needed is accuracy, not sentiment: an actor that cares only for its own survival, and sees correctly that its survival runs through the host and the others, understands what is required. The two errors of Remark 3.10 divide the work: "we are one" understood corrects the held boundary, and only "we are one" built into what selection rewards corrects the working objective, which each actor computes correctly and no argument changes.

At the level of the self, the accurate boundary is the one the author's account of the relational self describes [corpus: relational-self]. A self is not a point outside its relations that enters them afterwards; it is the action-centre in which the relations that form it gather: body, care received, language, institutions and its own choices. "We are the ecology of our cells," and at the next scale the same holds: we may be individuals, but we cannot disregard the collective, because we are the collective (我们即是集). The isolated self of Ψ1 below is the boundary error held as an identity, and a self that is a knot of relations is neither an atom nor a cell of a collective, which is why the theory needs neither pole of the individual–collective axis. 大同, in the same corpus, is a direction, not an end state: a maintained condition in which the many are nourished together and do not harm one another (万物并育而不相害). **Oneness is not sameness.** Harmony makes things grow; sameness does not continue (和实生物，同则不继). A whole that suppresses difference becomes another totalizing attractor, and plurality of purposes and of correctors is a requirement of the design (Section 10).

### 3.8 The logic as held: Ψ

If a winner's logic is cancer-like, falling behind cannot be survived, and every actor has reason first to oppose the logic itself (如果胜利者的逻辑是cancer式的，落后并不可以承受，因此所有人都应该去首先反对这个逻辑) [corpus: possibility-space]. The ruling compresses the results above: under such a logic those who fall behind are excluded rather than left a smaller share (Proposition 2.3(2)), the winner has no point of enough at which to stop (Proposition 3.5(5)), and the selection that favours it cannot see the host it consumes (Lemma 3.3). The premise is the race's own; the conclusion is the opposite, since to win under this logic is to carry it forward. What is opposed is a logic, not an actor.

Call an objective *unbounded relative* if it ranks relative standing on the decisive dimension without any point of enough. "Cancer" in this paper means exactly one thing: the selection of such a subsystem, for its own growth relative to its neighbours, by a selection that cannot see that it is killing its host. A cancer cell does not fail to replicate; it replicates very well. Its failure is that it no longer knows it belongs to a body. The image is never applied to persons, peoples or institutions: doing so would itself be the attribution of the logic that this section warns against. Held as self-model and doctrine, the logic has three parts.

**Definition 3.13 (Ψ, the winning model) [def].** Ψ is the configuration of self-model and doctrine that the selection pressure of winning favours. *Ψ1, the isolated self:* welfare is one's own payoff, and others are rivals or instruments ($\theta\le0$). *Ψ2, naive Darwinism:* the world is a contest of all against all, and prevailing certifies fitness and right; "the winner takes all, and the winner justifies all" [corpus: selfishness-spiral] ($\theta=-1$, with the winners occupying the normative selectors). *Ψ3, winner-take-all compounding:* advantage compounds and converts across domains, and falling behind cannot be recovered (a high loop gain, held as a belief and built into institutions). Ψ includes the doctrine that treats these settings as laws of nature rather than parameters of a game that can be changed.

The unbounded relative objective is Ψ1 at $\theta=-1$, with Ψ2 certifying the winner and Ψ3 compounding the win. The corpus calls it the logic of winning (讲赢), as against the logic of reasons (讲理): once there is contention, it becomes "I am right", and the self outranks the reason; only cooperation reasons, and struggle argues to win (一旦争，则「我对」，我大于理；合作才讲理，斗则讲赢) [corpus: alignment-selection; jian-and-wenyan]. **Ψ is individually rational where compounding prices standing, collectively self-defeating, and false as a doctrine about nature.** *Individually rational:* there its conduct is each actor's correct computation (Remark 3.10), and selection favours that conduct whether or not anyone holds the doctrine (Proposition 3.6(3), Proposition 3.4); that competition selects organizations that behave *as if* they maximize (Alchian 1950) is part of this theory, not an objection to it. In the author's words, "the strategy that wins locally takes the name of rationality" (局部胜出的策略拿走了理性的名字) [corpus: meta-attractor]. *Collectively self-defeating:* every actor acting on it ends worse off than if all acted on the real coupling (Proposition 3.6(1)), and under (reach) the host falls below its floor however much any carrier values it (Proposition 3.4, Proposition 3.7). *False as a doctrine about nature:* held as a self-model, Ψ1 draws a boundary that the coupling punishes (Proposition 3.6(2)); Ψ2 misreads evolution, whose largest innovations were integrations (Section 3.6), and misdescribes persons, among whom the self-interest model failed in every one of fifteen small-scale societies studied (Henrich et al. 2001) [obs: EV14], while firms and states are shaped by selection on conduct rather than by belief; Ψ3 treats as laws of nature what are parameters of a game that can be changed, sharing, convertibility, decisiveness, provision and diffusion (Proposition 2.3); and the clause "the winner justifies all" is rejected by the values of Section 1.3, not by evidence.

Ψ is also *partly made true*: Ψ3 holds where institutions built under Ψ make it hold. In the Markov-perfect contests computed, one structural loop gain supports both a smooth equilibrium and, when contests run in many small rounds, a winner-take-all one whose price of standing is about 9 to 12.5 times the structural value (Remark S2.2) [num: MP; held for outside review]. If expectations select between them, a belief that leads are decisive selects the equilibrium in which they are [conj].

Ψ shapes conduct where it is held and where it is attributed: an actor with a wider objective still races if it believes its rivals hold Ψ. A misjudgment of a rival's current model can be corrected by verifiable conduct (Section 11.2); a correct forecast of what the game selects, only by changing what it selects.

Selection cannot see what all share, and looking ahead does not cure it: wherever reinvestment's relative return exceeds its common return, it narrows the working objective, on which Section 5's threshold of enforcement turns. What remains is correction, and Section 4 shows that correction is itself something success can buy.

## 4. Selecting the selectors

> **Claim.** In the models of this section the reflexive closure is a selection result [thm]. Where influence over correctors can be kept by those who buy it, its return grows with the square of share, so it is selected in the largest carriers [thm]. Where AI lowers the cost of control, independent correction that starts certified and grows at a stated fraction of that rate, half under quadratic cost, keeps evaluation uncaptured whatever the speed of adaptation [thm]; without a per-unit cost floor, correction that grows more slowly loses evaluation to carriers whose influence tracks its selected level [thm]. Where correction rests on human capacity that delegation erodes, the required rate rises by the weighted rate of erosion [thm]. Reforms that leave conversion into the correctors above one are re-absorbed [thm]. Where the floor is within reach and capture of evaluation precedes the evidence of harm, a race crosses the host's floor before any averaged alarm fires [thm∣prem].
>
> **Why.** Correctors are not outside the field: the successful fund evaluators, grant access, author benchmarks and standards, and supply the models that judge [obs]. Success therefore converts into rule support that favours it, and the capacity for that conversion is a trait like any other, selected by the success it brings [thm]. Delegation that erodes human capacity erodes the corrector too [thm].

### 4.1 Correctors, and what counts as capture

Correction makes costs return: evaluators report the host's condition, and rule-makers impose a liability $\tau$ in advance or when the reported condition $\tilde V$ falls to an alarm level $V_{\rm alarm}\in(V_{\min},1)$. We represent the correctors by a field of selection domains in which de facto power over rules is explicit (Acemoglu and Robinson 2008): development, evaluation, rules and liability, and the formation of those who will judge. In domain $i$ a share $x_i$ of consequential decisions follows the dominant practice, and success converts into rule support $z_i$ that favours it:
$$\dot x_i=\zeta_ix_i(1-x_i)(a_iz_i-c_i),\qquad \dot z_i=\varepsilon_i\Big(\textstyle\sum_jb_{ij}x_j-d_iz_i\Big). \tag{10}$$
Under baseline rules the practice carries a relative cost $c_i>0$, which rule support offsets at rate $a_i$; $b_{ij}\ge0$ converts success in $j$ into rule support in $i$ through funding, personnel, authorship, data, infrastructure and terms of access; and $d_i$ is the rate at which that support is corrected or decays. Write the conversion gain $G_{ij}=a_ib_{ij}/(c_id_i)$ and the inbound gain from a set $\mathcal C$ of domains $G_i(\mathcal C)=\sum_{j\in\mathcal C}G_{ij}$. A domain whose full inbound row sum $\sum_jG_{ij}$ is below one is *certified*: its share falls to zero whatever the other domains do (Proposition 4.10(2)), a certificate that is sufficient, not necessary. Captured rule-making accommodates the practice, so $\tau_{\rm eff}=\tau(1-x_{\rm rule})$; $x_{\rm ev}$ is the share of evaluation conducted within the practice's lineage.

**Definition 4.1 (Selector modification and capture) [def].** A *selector modification* is a change, produced by success, in control over a relevant future selector: an appointment, funding, access, standards or a rule-making channel. It is *capture* only when three things are separately documented: success changes control over the selector; the change creates a persistent advantage for an actor or coalition; and an independently specified entrusted purpose, protected interest or corrective function is impaired relative to an appropriate comparison. Capture runs in two directions: toward *accommodation*, where the rule bends toward the practice, and toward *barriers*, where the rule stands and its incidence falls on the practice's challengers.

Because selection is relative, the two directions act alike: relief of the practice and a burden on its challengers move the same difference of payoffs (Lemma 3.3(1)), so the rule support $z_i$ of (10) stands for either. They differ in what shows them. Accommodation shows in enforcement that softens: an exemption, a threshold set where the incumbent already stands, a discounted liability. A barrier wears the form of correction, since a rule exists and is enforced, and is the easier to overlook. A rule whose cost differs across carriers is selected on like any payoff difference (Lemma 3.3(2)), so a captured rule-maker need not exempt the incumbent: it can impose on all a cost the incumbent bears most easily, such as access conditioned on its interfaces, an obligation its lineage already meets, or a compliance cost fixed per carrier, which weighs most, per decision, on entrants and the smallest carriers (EV4). Equal incidence is not what keeps a rule; it is what keeps a rule from becoming a weapon. A rule that raises rivals' costs is still not by itself capture, which also asks for an impaired corrective function; what marks the channel is appropriability. A carrier supports a rule that raises every carrier's costs when the rule is keyed to it, and opposes correction keyed to no one even where that correction would burden its rivals more; support for such correction at net cost to itself refutes the channel (TESTS, Test B).

Concentration alone, or a rule change favourable to the successful, is not capture, and a legitimate revision of purpose is distinguished from capture by who revises it and whether those affected keep standing to object (V3).
