Where prior trust falls short, $1-\mu<\varpi^\ast$, the receiver restrains after the step with probability at most $c_{\mathsf R}/\Pi_{\mathsf R}$ in every equilibrium; where $c_{\mathsf S}\le\Pi_{\mathsf S}c_{\mathsf R}/\Pi_{\mathsf R}$ the bound is attained, by an equilibrium in which $\mathsf R$ imitates just often enough to hold the receiver's belief at $\varpi^\ast$.*
3. *A step that is reversible or unverifiable, $c_{\mathsf R}\to0$, is therefore cheap talk: it buys no restraint that prior trust did not already give.*

*Scope.* Whether the step separates is a question of measured costs. Where the lock is certain, keeping AI out raises the survival-first type's own survival (Proposition 6.4(1)), so $c_{\mathsf S}$ can be zero or negative and that type takes the step whatever the receiver does; a relative type delegates at every speed (Proposition 6.2(3)), so a durable removal costs it the speed it forgoes for the period, and the longer the period, the larger $c_{\mathsf R}$. Where prior trust falls short and the inequalities of (1) are strict, the separating equilibrium is the only one that survives the intuitive criterion. *Proof:* S11 [num: TR].

Unilateral, verifiable narrow E1 is therefore a candidate first move: cheap for an actor that prices in the lock, costly for one that values the speed delegation buys, which is the difference a signal needs [thm∣prem]. It is the costly signal by which a security-seeking type reveals itself (Kydd 2000), and only inspection of doctrine and exercises keeps a relative type from declaring it without taking it (S11). It answers a misjudgment of a rival's present objective; a correct forecast of what the game will select is answered only by changing what it selects (Section 3.8).

### 11.3 Four temporal arrangements, and readiness

Keeping AI out of the contest, stopping, slowing and pausing are different choices, with different costs, different verification and different failures. Section 5.3 defined stopping (E2) and slowing (E3) by what they do to the growth channel of risk; here each is stated as an arrangement.

**E1: AI out of the contest.** *The narrow form* keeps human authority over the decisions whose delegation would lock with near certainty: strategic and escalatory decisions that are irreversible within the time available for deliberation, such as the use of force and nuclear command. It comes with jointly verified limits, drilled fallback capacity, and an evidence channel independent of AI, so that a human judging an alarm has reasons of their own. Among actors who price in the lock it is a strict equilibrium that needs no trust where the lock is certain, $q_L=1$, and it stays one while the lock fails with a probability below the bound of Remark S6.1 (Proposition 6.4(1)) [thm]. Where the lock is uncertain, as in tactical domains with a large speed advantage, delegation can be an equilibrium too (Proposition 6.4(2), (3)), and keeping AI out rests on a commitment held in common, or on the signal of Proposition 11.2 where its costs separate; bounded, reversible defensive automation, such as response in cyber defence within stated limits, falls under the test of O2 (Section 6.6), not under E1. *The broad form* is the scope: AI not an instrument in any contest that tends toward dominance or concentration, in labour, finance and persuasion as much as over shared conditions, judged by the game and not by the act; Section 6.6 gives its criterion and the three layers of its enforcement. **Broad E1 is the scope; narrow E1 is the first step** [corpus: alignment-selection, 2026-09-26]. E1 is held in common. An actor that stays out of the contest alone while others race survives less than by joining wherever staying out raises a rival's chance of a decisive position enough (Proposition S8.1(3); X11) [thm], which is why E1 begins with the decision loops in which the lock makes it self-enforcing, and is extended by agreement.

**The deterrence objection.** A commitment that cannot be revoked is worth more than one that can (Schelling 1960), and at its strongest, semi-automated retaliation would relieve the pressure to launch on warning. Survivable, human-controlled forces give the same relief without removing judgment; the commitment value of automation is the lock itself, O2 given up in that domain (Lemma 6.3). An automated commitment cannot tell a false alarm from an attack, and false alarms have been stopped by human judgment, as in 1983, when an officer judged an alarm false and did not report an attack (Podvig 2002; Hoffman 2009) [obs: EV18]. Escalation at machine speed is a common risk that relative selection does not see (Lemma 3.3), so the contest under-prices it [thm∣prem]. X11 gives the answers in full.

**E2: stop.** Capability of the decisive class is not built while the conditions for governing it are absent: an accurate model of the coupling, the shared prediction, and common custody. Where hazardous variety already exceeds coverage, it is rolled back, which ends exposure fastest (Proposition 5.14). For divergences that hide under evaluation, E2 and E3 are what lies beyond admission (Remark 6.8). Because E2 applies to the decisive class only, its cost, if AI turns out not to be decisive, is the delay of that class's margin over capable systems that are not decisive, including lives that earlier benefits would have saved. The cost can be large (crux C10) and, for those who bear it, irreversible; it does not foreclose later choice, whereas if AI is decisive the loss it avoids forecloses choice for everyone. The asymmetry of irreversibility concerns the option set, not the size of the losses (Arrow and Fisher 1974). Coverage of E2 comes from consent or from coercion; coercion's risk of escalation is counted in $\Theta$, and the conditions under which a coercive restriction of frontier inputs enters even the comparison of designs are stated once, in Section 12.1.

**E3: develop at the pace of understanding.** In each hazard class, capability grows no faster than contestable coverage can follow, so the uncovered hazardous share stays summable (Theorem 5.13) and each hazard mode keeps its robustness margin (Proposition 5.12). It binds class by class, where coverage falls behind, and it binds a world that has stopped racing (Section 5.3); its carriers are thresholds in capability and in effective compute tied to measured coverage per class (Section 10.4). How much it slows depends on whether AI-assisted oversight raises coverage as fast as hazardous variety grows (crux C3a). Stopping is not slowing: E2 forgoes the decisive class, and E3 paces each hazard class to its coverage.

**A temporary pause** is the fourth arrangement, and by itself it changes nothing.

**Proposition 11.3 (A pause is valued by what changes during it) [thm].** A pause that changes nothing buys time, not survival. *Let $h_t$ be the residual hazard of the irreversible event in the $t$-th active round, and let a pause add no hazard of its own. (1) If the same process resumes after the pause, so that the $t$-th active round has the same $h_t$, survival over any number of active rounds is unchanged, and so is its limit, which is zero where the $h_t$ are not summable (Lemma 5.11). (2) A pause after which the residual sequence is summable, where before it was not, keeps survival bounded away from zero. (3) In the field model, a pause that freezes shares while ordinary rule support keeps accumulating completes capture from every frozen share above $1/G$ (Proposition 4.12).*

*Proof:* S11 [num: TR].

A pause buys time to change the causal conditions: verification, residual hazards, human capacity, enforceable limits, shared provision, objectives and institutions. An indefinite stop, a lasting cap, or a pause that produces a different decision process is a different policy. **A pause with no relevant change is not a certificate of readiness to resume**, and readiness has therefore to be defined.

**Definition 11.4 (Readiness to resume) [def].** *Readiness is assessed along seven dimensions, each with observable indicators; a threshold set by the risk decisions of the domain of use, with the error of measurement inside it; and an assessor whose own row sums are certified and who is insulated from rule-making during the pause. A dimension is met when its indicator has stayed past its threshold for its residence time, at least the time constant of the slow variable on which the dimension rests. Each dimension is decided by a sequential test registered in advance with two error rates: the rate of declaring it met when it is not, set by the protection standard, since resuming too early is the error that cannot be undone; and the rate of declaring it unmet when it is met, bounded so that the seven together hold back a ready state with no more than a registered probability. A met dimension is re-opened by a test of the same kind, not by a single reading. All seven must be met; none compensates for another.*

| Dimension | Question | Slow variable; the residence time is at least |
|---|---|---|
| Q1 Technical reliability | Has the residual hazard of the relevant classes fallen, by evidence independent of the developer and valid against evaluation awareness? | Residual risk per class; the class's manifestation window $\tau_e$ (Proposition 5.12) |
| Q2 Shared objectives | Have governing purposes moved from relative advantage toward shared provision? | The self-models that governing positions select; a turnover of those positions |
| Q3 Strategic incentives | Has the value of winning fallen relative to the joint value? | The won share of capacity, $\omega$ (Proposition 5.17); a period of growing stakes long enough to show provision keeping pace with them |
| Q4 Effective correction | Can evaluators obtain evidence, keep independent funding and execute findings? | Rule support in each corrector domain; its dwell (Proposition S11.2), not the return of shares to their former level |
| Q5 Deployment constraints | Is the tempo of capability and delegation bound to correction capacity? | Coverage against hazardous variety; a generation of capability, across which catch rates of planted defects at fixed human hours stay level |
| Q6 Retained human agency | Can people still take over delegated functions? | Human capacity $\mathcal M$; its recovery time after delegation (Lemma 6.3) |
| Q7 Custody and verifiability | Is decisive-class capability in common custody, and conduct verifiable faster than breakout? | The capability held in check, and detection latency against the time to a decisive position (Remark 5.5); a period in which the capability grows, since the net gain from exit grows with it (Proposition S8.5) |

These are dimensions to operationalize, not a checklist proved sufficient. They do not require that every person's private motives change first: binding institutions can change the effective game while private competitive preferences persist, which is why effective correction is among them. Readiness does not ask for four rows of Table 9.1, R1, R2, R7 and R8b (Table S9.2); it presupposes them, since no pause supplies them. The residence condition is there because levels move before slow variables cross: shares fall back while rule support still sits in the basin of capture (Section 11.4). The decision rule is there because noise and conjunction together bias a fixed reading toward "not ready": seven dimensions, each read once with a 5% chance of missing a met dimension, pass a ready state with probability $0.95^7\approx0.70$ [thm]; a pause held past its evidence raises the cost of staying, and coverage tips once that cost exceeds the cost of standing outside (Remark S9.1) [thm∣prem]. A sequential test turns noise into delay instead of bias, at error rates fixed in advance (Remark S11.1) [num: TR]. Readiness is the release rule of the second tier of Section 12.1, read on indicators of its own rather than on those of activation run backwards; X11 records where each dimension stands today. Resuming unchanged after a pause would repeat the process that made the pause necessary.

**The order.** By the trust each needs, E1 comes first, since it keeps humans as the players and, in its narrow form, is a strict equilibrium among actors who price in the lock; then E2 or E3, class by class: stopping before the decisive class is built, or bringing development below the speed at which understanding and response can follow. By verifiability the order reverses. E2 and E3 are the most verifiable among rivals, since chips, fabs, datacentres and compute can be observed, though thresholds stated in compute erode: with pre-training efficiency doubling about every eight months (90% interval about two to twenty-two months, on data to 2023), a nominal compute cap must tighten roughly two and a half to three times a year at the central estimate, a rate condition that verification must meet (EV9) [obs]; hardware mechanisms for verifying compute use are proposed and prototyped, not deployed (EV17) [obs]. E1 is the hardest to verify, since it concerns software and internal procedure, and the most self-enforcing among actors who price in the lock, which is why it can begin as declared doctrine with inspected exercises. A third order, by time constant, decides what is started first, and the next subsection gives it.

### 11.4 Parallel tracks, dwell and windows

Each commitment of the transition moves a slow variable, named in the third column of the table below, and holds only if that variable crosses its threshold before the commitment is withdrawn (Proposition 4.11). The slow variables have different time constants, and a slow variable that is not moving when it is needed cannot be moved then. Started one after another in the order of the trust they need, the slowest would start last and finish after the window it serves had closed. The commitments are therefore tracks that run in parallel, started in the order of their time constants, slowest first, and binding in the order of the trust they need, from the first track to the fifth [def]. The tracks are a candidate to be tested, not a claim that every setting follows them: civilian services, frontier development and strategic decisions can need different paths (X11).

| Track | What is committed | Slow variable and its time constant | Mechanisms acted on | Compensating loop to anticipate | Indicator of progress |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1. Shared account of hazards | A common taxonomy; shared measurement of coverage and residual risk per class, kept apart from measurement of who leads; audited disclosure of conversion data | Shared understanding of the hazards, which cannot run ahead of the evidence: for late-manifesting classes, their manifestation window (Proposition 5.12) | Language; masking and attribution | Measurement of hazards turned into measurement of who leads; capture of the measurers | Agreement among independent measurements; their misses uncorrelated |
| 2. Narrow, testable commitments | Narrow E1, beginning with declared doctrine and inspected exercises; an incident channel; pre-deployment access above thresholds | Human capacity to take delegated decisions back, which decays at $\delta_h$ under delegation and recovers at $\varrho_h$ without it (Lemma 6.3) | Decisiveness; attribution | Delegation moved outside the commitment; cheap imitation of the signal | Inspected exercises; catch rates of planted errors in drills |
| 3. Fund correction and shared services | A formula levy; evaluators with mandated access; services with continuity guarantees; reward separated from authority over correction | Rule support in each corrector domain: several institutional turnovers $1/(\varepsilon d)$, more the deeper the lock-in (Proposition S11.2) | Selecting the selectors; accounting | The formula becomes a prize; capture moves to the funders; reformers are selected out on the way to office | Row-sum certificates; lineage shares |
| 4. Delegation, access, custody, decision rights | Broad E1; tempo thresholds; common custody; common frontier projects | The capability held in check, which raises the net gain from exit (Proposition S8.5); the lead time of custody's installation and verification, which must be shorter than the time to a decisive position | Compounding; tempo; decisiveness | Leakage to unconstrained actors; overhang under the cap; competition moving to unshared dimensions | Conduct under sharing; overhang against the hazard of exit |
| 5. Broaden as institutions prove themselves | Expansion by evidence: requirements and durability tests met under stress | The record of the arrangements under stress, and the self-models formed inside them: a cycle of stress and recovery, and for minds a generation | The boundary of the system | Closure into a bloc; after a recovery, mobilization falls back and certificates can fail (Remark 4.7) | Openness of membership; standing of non-members |

Slowest first means that the third and fourth tracks begin before the trust they will bind on exists: rule support in the corrector domains moves slowest, custody has to be prepared further ahead of need than any warning gives, and the openness of the fifth track has to be built in from the first. That is why Section 12.1 begins its first two tiers together.

**Dwell.** A reform that insulates rule-making from shares lets rule support decay at its rate of correction, but shares answer to rule support and lag it: they keep rising while support stays above break-even, for $\ln G/(\varepsilon d)$, so the reform has to be held through that rise and then long enough to repay the lock-in it added; from a captured share above $x_\sigma$, shares are back at their starting level before the state has left the basin of capture [thm]. A rollback judged on share levels is therefore premature (Test D). What decides is where rule support stands against the separatrix, which is not observed directly, and near full share the depth of lock-in cannot be read from the level at all. From a captured share of 0.99 the hold that is needed runs, in units of $1/(\varepsilon d)$, from 1.89 to 10.45 as $G$ rises from 2 to 10 when rule-making is ten times slower than adoption, and from 5.86 to 16.18 when the two are equally fast [num: DW]. It grows with the depth of lock-in, and depth grows with the age of capture, so without a floor under the alternative the hold has no bound [thm]: at equal speeds and $G=2$ it is 5.86 from a share of 0.99 and 28.9 from $1-10^{-12}$ [num: DW]. A lineage of the alternative that persists inside selection, and is recruited when the rule changes, bounds it [thm]: with $G=10$ and rule-making ten times slower, a floor of 0.1% cuts the hold from 10.45 to 3.81, while a share protected outside selection, which does not recruit, leaves it at 10.44 (Proposition S11.2) [num: DW]. These holds take the conversion gain as fixed during the reform; where capture has built capacity that goes on converting, the gain stays higher and so does the hold. For real institutions the magnitudes are a conjecture [conj].

That is the mechanism of decay and renewal. In the field model, an order in which forms can fail and be replaced can be reformed in bounded time, and one that has frozen cannot [thm]. In the author's words, "look at the ecosystem, death is not a bug but a feature, changes and mutations are what keeps things alive — create a rich space of systems, not reducing to one rule, one attractor" [corpus: shengsheng, 2026-06-05]. This is the second job of plurality that Section 10.4 names: lineages kept inside selection and able to grow make reform possible in bounded time, as well as decorrelating errors.

**The ratchet.** In the field model, graduated steps reach the threshold only as a ratchet [thm]. Each step has to carry its own slow variable across its threshold and be held there for its dwell, since a step that moves a parameter without crossing is re-absorbed (Proposition 4.11) and one released on levels is premature; a sequence of steps none of which crosses is a sequence of re-absorptions, whatever trust it builds, and a step that crosses on one track does not cross on another (Remark S11.3). A transition is not shown to arrive by small steps that each feel safe; it arrives, if it does, by steps each of which is kept.

**Windows.** Whether frontier development is still the keystone, the domain whose reform would take down the captured domains outside the remaining core, is a conjecture [conj]. The window closes as conversion among the corrector domains grows (Section 4.5), which is why the correction track cannot wait for the trust that custody needs. Arrangements of this kind are often adopted when a crisis opens a window (Gunderson and Holling 2002) [obs]. A design prepared in advance, with its activation committed, is what a crisis can adopt; one adopted in a crisis must state what would end each emergency closure before adoption, or it will not afterwards. Smaller jurisdictions, users and those who depend on services controlled by others need standing on every track, because they cannot leave the shared exposure.

**Where things stand.** The first track has seeds in an international scientific assessment and a UN scientific panel, which has issued a first report and whose appointment one leading state opposed, calling the panel and the global dialogue beside it an overreach. The second has seeds in an affirmation by two leading states of human control over nuclear use and a bilateral dialogue with an incident channel, while the most recent multilateral declaration on military AI was endorsed by neither leading state and the most recent General Assembly resolution on it was opposed by one; narrow E1 has no multilateral document that both leading states now endorse, and its only shared basis is that bilateral affirmation. Meanwhile one leading state's official rejected a drift toward global governance, and two developers' written commitments were made contingent on rivals (EV2, EV7, EV8) [obs]. The slow tracks, correction and custody, have no seed of comparable standing (EV3, EV17) [obs]. The seeds must become binding, verifiable and broader before the relevant losses or lock-in.

### 11.5 Requirements of a reachable path, and precedents

**Table 11.5 (Requirements of a reachable path) [def, with support: each row carries the tag of the result it cites].** *A path that changes what is selected has four separate requirements, and what is shown for each differs. The table organizes results proved elsewhere; it does not show that the four can be met together.*

| Requirement | What it asks | Support | Not shown |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Existence** | An end state that every participant prefers to keep: an equilibrium of the changed game, viable once in force | Under a common frontier, all cooperating is a strict equilibrium beside the race while the value of winning is moderate (Proposition 10.1) [num]; an overwhelmingly strong actor takes part only for a share near its race payoff (Proposition S10.3) [thm]; the safeguards give viability once in force, with a margin that the host sets (Theorem 10.4) [thm] | That the cooperative equilibrium is the one selected; the pooled effort it needs rises steeply with the value of winning |
| **Reachability** | Every participant prefers each step, given its beliefs | In objectives, trust at least the assurance threshold (Proposition 8.6), which Corollary 11.1 lowers [thm]; in survival terms, none for an actor that holds the prediction where restraint survives better against both conducts, at the horizons where it does (Proposition S8.1(2), (5)) [thm]; none for narrow E1 among actors who price in a certain lock (Proposition 6.4(1)) [thm]; a costly signal where a rival's objective is unknown (Proposition 11.2) [thm]; graduated steps only as a ratchet (Remark S11.3) [thm∣prem] | That graduated steps alone reach the threshold |
| **Survival of the transient** | The host survives the transition, and no point of no return is passed first | The liability margin of Theorem 10.4(1) [thm]; human capacity kept above its lock-in floor (Lemma 6.3) [thm]; the uncovered share summable while the transition lasts (Theorem 5.13) [thm∣prem]; custody before decisiveness, which meets R4 by changing the premise of enforcement (Corollary 5.7) [thm]; the host's clock and the capture clock compared, not assumed (Proposition 4.13) [thm∣prem] | The margin outside the hosts computed; that the two clocks fall in the right order on the present path, which is a condition to measure |
| **Persistence** | Each step held until it is not re-absorbed, and the arrangement not undone by success | Commitment that keeps pace with the net gain from exit (Proposition S8.5) [thm]; correctors, rivals' verifiers included, with full row sums below one (Proposition 4.10(2); Proposition S10.2) [thm]; rules fixed before capture, and each step held for its dwell, which a recruitable lineage bounds (Proposition 4.11; Proposition S11.2) [thm; num]; coverage above its participation threshold (Remark S9.1) [thm∣prem] | Durability under stress (crux C4); that real institutions have the model's dwell [conj] |

Read as a problem of control, the table asks whether the present state lies in the set from which the target can be reached, and then held, under admissible steps: the capture basin of viability theory (Aubin 1991). That is proved only in the one-domain field model, where the hold of Section 11.4 is the minimum capture time; the basin for several domains, with the host's floor and with disturbances, is not computed (Remark S11.4).

**Precedents.** The record of restraint among rivals qualifies all of this, and each precedent's limiting condition is where its analogy to AI is weakest (X11, corrected against the sources) [obs]. The 1946 proposals for international ownership of atomic energy failed on sequence and on enforcement against violators: **custody must precede decisiveness**. The Biological Weapons Convention shows that a weapon endangering its holder can be renounced unilaterally, where its military value was low and the renouncer held another deterrent, and that without verification a covert programme can persist. Nuclear power after a serious accident shows durable safety under unchanged motives where the shared loss returns to its source and no prize is decisive, with later lapses: a change of selection, not of motive. The Montreal Protocol shows coverage made attractive by provision and enforced by conditions of access, once substitutes were available and the value of winning was low. Rivals survived where nature or balancing supplied saturation (Section 7.2); if advantage in AI is unsaturated, as the racers' own premise holds (crux C5), safety must come from changing what is selected before advantage becomes decisive [thm∣prem].

Section 12 takes from here the release rule of its second tier, readiness held for its residence time and judged against the dwell, and the reason its slowest preparations are warranted now: what binds late has to begin early.

## 12. The judgment

> **Claim.** Under the protection standard and the present evidence, a change in what the system selects for is warranted in three tiers, sorted by the kind of justification each step has, not by who would accept it [val]. Steps whose justification waits on no crux are taken now, and beside them steps warranted until a named crux is measured favourable [rec]. The substance of the change, stopping the decisive class (E2), common custody and development at the pace of understanding (E3), is prepared now and activated by a mechanism committed in advance, on registered indicators estimated by correctors whose independence is certified [rec]. Whole designs, the coercive restriction of frontier inputs among them, are compared under one range of uncertainty, each with its own failure modes; where every worst case crosses the tolerance, as each does under the rule for that range, the ranking is a judgment at the standard's third step [val].
>
> **Why.** The first tier keeps options open and measures the cruxes: measurement, preparation whose lead time no warning covers, equilibria without trust and research that is a public good hold on every reading [val]. Custody has to exist before anyone could hold decisive capability alone, and the trigger fires where restraint is least enforceable (Proposition 5.4(4)), so an activation decided then would face the condition it is meant to prevent [thm∣prem]. At the third step what can be compared is which failures can be reversed, into what and by whom [val]. One failure is irreversible on every path, one party coming to hold decisive capability: the race selects for it, a design built to Section 10 contests it, and the rest of that design's failures, the escalation of a coercive fallback apart, have named reversals, none yet shown to work [thm∣prem; val].

### 12.1 The judgment, in tiers

The judgment applies the protection standard of Section 8.1 to the present evidence. Its tiers are kinds of justification, not stages: the first two begin together, and what has the longest time constant starts first (Section 11.4).

**Tier 1(a). Warranted now, on every reading of the cruxes [rec].** A step belongs here only if its justification does not depend on how any crux comes out: measurement that decides the cruxes, preparation whose lead time exceeds any warning, a step that is an equilibrium without trust, or research that is a public good.
1. **Shared measurement** of hazards, coverage and residual risk per class, by predefined hazard modes and the four conditions of Section 5.3, kept apart from measurement of who leads, with masked classes measured adversarially; audited disclosure, from every party, of conversion into correctors (funding, appointment, access, the authorship of standards), with a sanction above the gain from omission (Lemma 3.3(3)); certified row sums for every corrector domain (Proposition 4.10(2)); the correlation of evaluators' misses on common test sets, and control evaluations in which the red team knows the protocol (Proposition 6.9); and, wherever models evaluate models, the share of evaluation done by the evaluated lineage (Remark 4.9).
2. **Verification research and pilots**: of compute, training and custody, and of detection faster than breakout.
3. **Alignment and control research, commonly funded and openly published.** What lowers the alignment tax is a public good that competitors undersupply (Lemma 3.11) [thm∣prem]; once published it is common to all, and where it brings a class's tax to zero or below, the safeguard spreads without coordination (Section 6.4).
4. **Preparation of common custody**: its design, institutions, indicators and activation rule registered now, and the commitment to activate taken now.
5. **Narrow E1**, for the decisions whose delegation would lock with near certainty: strategic and escalatory decisions irreversible within the time available for deliberation. It begins with declared doctrine and inspected exercises, with drilled human fallback and an evidence channel independent of AI, and is verified wherever verification is possible (Section 11.3). Among actors who price in a certain lock it is best whatever the others do, a strict equilibrium that needs no trust (Proposition 6.4(1)), and "near certainty" has an exact bound (Remark S6.1) [thm]; taken verifiably by one actor alone, it is the candidate first move of Proposition 11.2, a signal wherever durability makes it cost a relative actor at least what the restraint it induces is worth to that actor [thm].

TESTS sets each item against every crux, and each survives for a reason of its own kind. Measurement is worth most where readings disagree, and every trigger of tier 2 rests on it. An instrument of verification has to exist before the deviation it is to detect, since detection in time is the enforcement condition seen from verification (Remark 5.5) [thm]. Research that lowers the tax pays whatever the tax turns out to be. Where decisive advantage never arrives, preparing custody costs only the preparation; where it arrives, custody begun at a warning is in place too late wherever building it takes longer than the warning gives (Section 7.2) [thm∣prem].

**Tier 1(b). Warranted now; withdrawn only where a named crux is measured favourable [rec].** Each item names its crux and, where a measurement can withdraw only part of the item, which part. The burden of proof follows the protection standard, because what these items guard against arrives before the evidence that would show it: an alarm averaged over captured evaluators fires late or not at all (Proposition 4.8); where capture precedes evidence, the host crosses its floor before any alarm (Proposition 4.13); and a masked divergence is detected only as often as it shows under evaluation (Remark 6.8) [thm∣prem]. A protection that waits for proof of what it protects against waits for proof that comes too late, so each item stands until its crux is measured favourable, not only after it is measured unfavourable [val].
6. **Independent correction**: formula funding at arm's length, mandated access, rotation, and rule-making insulated from the success of the evaluated, growing at least at the rate the rate law requires, and faster by $w\delta_h$ where it rests on human capacity that delegation erodes (Proposition 4.5(2); Proposition 4.6(1)) [thm]. Withdrawn beyond present institutions where row sums are measured below one under present funding and access, with findings executed against leaders (C2; Test M). The measurement of item 1 continues even then, since a certificate valid today fails later where correction falls below that rate and capacity tracks its selected level [thm].
7. **Common admission constraints**, before consequential deployment, internal deployment included, for every class of divergence whose costs fall on common terms: sanctioned at least at every type's gain from evasion, which keeps the divergence out, and of equal incidence among those who comply, which keeps the constraint neutral among them (R8a, R8b of Table 9.1), with audits for hidden objectives and loyalties (Section 6.3). Withdrawn for a class whose tax is measured zero or negative, since its safeguard then pays privately (C9). For masked classes, admission is by capability threshold, up to the capability at which control protocols that do not assume the system's cooperation remain safe, with tier 2 beyond it (Remark 6.8); that part gives way to admission on evidence the system's behaviour does not control, up to the capability at which independent auditors are shown to bound masking (C12).
8. **Shared provision of useful services**, good enough, distributed and replaceable, **and the divisible gains of capability shared as they arise**, where they can begin. They realize joint value on every reading of the cruxes (V1, V5) [val]. Provision not won in the contest lowers the won share on which the scissors turn (Proposition 5.17), and gains shared as they arise, unlike a promise for after victory, do not rest on a winner's restraint (Proposition 5.8) [thm]. What a measurement can withdraw is the reliance on them to change conduct: if conduct is unchanged under substantial sharing (C6), or provision leaves the weight on winning where it was (C8), that reliance moves to custody and pace, and the sharing stays for what it realizes [rec]. They are not accepted across camps, and the judgment does not claim that they are.

**Tier 2. Prepared now; activated by mechanism on registered indicators [rec].** A step whose justification turns on a crux that can be measured in time is prepared now and activated by mechanism. This tier carries the substance of the change: stopping the decisive class (E2), with rollback where hazardous variety already exceeds coverage; common custody of capability of the decisive class, in force; and development at the pace of understanding (E3), for each hazard class whose coverage falls behind its variety. Stopping is not slowing: E2 concerns the decisive class and E3 the pace of each hazard class, each activated on its own indicators (Section 11.3). The premise of the race is the reason to prepare them now (Section 7.2).
- *Trigger.* **A trigger fires when the estimated time to a decisive position, less the time custody needs to be installed and verified, falls below a margin registered in advance**, and custody and E2 activate. Its indicators are the persistence of leads, read on every measure since leads differ by measure, the gap between open and closed models (C5), and detection latency against the time from breakout to decisiveness (C7). E3 activates for a hazard class when its coverage falls behind: residual risk per class not falling, catch rates of planted defects at fixed human hours falling as capability rises (C1, C3a), and, for masked classes, independent auditors' detection at fixed access falling with capability (C12). Waiting until decisiveness is shown would be too late.
- *Estimator.* The time to a decisive position and the timeliness of detection are what masking hides and strategic disclosure shades (Remark 4.9; Remark 5.5). They are estimated by correctors whose row sums are certified (C2) and whose misses are measured for correlation (C3b), from channels that observe actions directly, such as compute, custody, deployments and the performance of contracts, with any bound on masking drawn from evidence the observed do not control [rec].
- *Commitment.* **Activation is committed in advance and executed by mechanism, not decided afresh when the trigger fires.** The trigger fires where restraint is least enforceable (Proposition 5.4(4)), as the breakout arm of Proposition 5.17 raises $p_d$, so a collective decision taken there would inherit the enforcement failure it was meant to prevent [thm∣prem]. Preparation shortens the delay of the response from building, deciding and detecting to deciding and detecting, and commitment leaves only detecting: that is the delay Proposition 5.12 bounds when the mode to be held is a lead that compounds [thm∣prem].
- *Release.* Release reads indicators of its own, not those of activation run backwards, and requires each to hold for the dwell time of the slow variable it rests on (Definition 11.4; Section 11.4): share levels recover before safety does, a result of the field model [thm] and a conjecture for institutions [conj], and capture that makes delegation look safe is harder to undo than to prevent (Proposition 4.6(2)) [num: QS]. A release read from the activation indicators would come too early.

**Tier 3. Compared, not settled [val].** The whole designs are the separately held configuration of Section 10.5, developed in X10; a polycentric complex of mutually recognized institutions; a verification agreement with shared provision; regulated competition that meets Table 9.1; an agreement of the type of Section 8.3(g); the benign winner; and the coercive restriction of frontier inputs. They are compared with continuation and with one another on the durability tests of Definition 8.4, in one range $\Theta$ that includes each one's own failure modes.

The coercive restriction of frontier inputs enters the comparison only under three conditions of its own, two set by mechanisms of Part I and one by value. Its burden is equal, the restrictor's own frontier work included, since a stop borne unequally re-sorts those it binds and is evaded wherever evasion pays their type (Lemma 3.3(2), (3)) [thm], and those it binds read it as a lead being kept. It is executed by correctors certified independent of every party it restricts and of the restrictor, with full inbound row sums below one, since otherwise the restrictor is the largest conversion target there is (Proposition 4.3) [thm]. And the chamber of those affected consents (V3; R7) [val]. Its escalation hazard is counted in $\Theta$ (Proposition 8.5). Even under the three conditions the theory does not derive that its net effect is positive: denial raises the weight on winning and the trust threshold, the hard case of Section 8.3(f).

Under the rule for $\Theta$ the worst case of every path includes an irreversible loss, so none meets the tolerance, and the ranking falls to the standard's third step: prefer the lowest bound that keeps options of correction open (Section 8.1). That step needs estimates on both sides, which the cruxes would supply and do not yet; the ranking is therefore a judgment, stated as one [val]. The preference for changing selection rests on two reasons, read from the failure modes that X8 lists path by path, each with its support.
- **(i) Reversibility over every failure mode.** A failure is reversible only if its reversal is named, stops short of O1 and O2, and is executed by someone other than the failing party. Read from its own documents, each of the design's failures but two has such a reversal: dissolution falls back into separately held functions, with the protections against unilateral domination entrenched; a function whose certificate fails loses executive force until recertified, its assets passing to several providers; an interrupted service is carried by the share cap, escrow and funded standby; collusion among the checks, once a measured correlation of misses exposes it, is undone by rotation and plural lineage; overreach of a common constraint ends at its sunset, its stated end condition or an appeal with the burden on the compact. None of these reversals has yet been shown to work; whether they do is C4 [conj]. The two exceptions are the escalation of its coercive fallback, which every coercive path carries, and the failure of (ii). Continuation's failures are reversible only while no position is decisive and no floor has been crossed. Its worst have no reversal: coverage outgrown and a floor crossed (Theorem 5.13), capture before evidence (Proposition 4.13), a decisive position reached (Proposition 5.8), the capacity to overrule lost (Lemma 6.3) [thm∣prem]; and for hazards that manifest late its premises cannot be measured before they bind (Proposition 5.12). The asymmetry is between reversals that are designed and not yet tested and losses that are derived and cannot be undone. That is what the third step means by keeping options of correction open: a state from which a named reversal, if it works against every error in $\Theta$, still avoids the protected losses lies in the correctable kernel (Definition 8.1), and a failure without one has left it [def].
- **(ii) The irreversible failure every path shares.** One failure has no reversal on any path: one party comes to hold decisive capability, by a decisive win on continuation, by restraint failing after victory for the benign winner, by a freeze broken after a sprint, by a covert project escaping a verified agreement, by the conversion of a restrictor's correctors, and, for the design, by capture of the custody of decisive capability. The race produces that failure by construction and selects for it (Proposition 7.3) [thm∣prem]; the design contests it by separation, plural lineages and the tests D4 and D5, and the guardian test of Remark 10.5 applies to custody first. The same loss stands on both sides of the comparison; what differs is whether a path selects for it or is built against it.

A third consideration waits on measurement. Among paths that measurement brings inside the tolerance, the standard's second step applies, greater useful benefit more fairly distributed, and there the design's case is the joint value it realizes and the race dissipates (Section 10.1); since no path is inside the tolerance in its worst case, it orders only the futures in which a crux is measured favourable. A reader who weighs these reasons differently can reach another ranking; Section 12.3 says which measurements would move it.

**The scope.** Broad E1, AI not used as an instrument in any contest that tends toward dominance or concentration, in labour, finance and persuasion as much as over shared conditions, is the scope of what has been argued; narrow E1 is the first step (Section 6.6). Between them the game itself is changed, by lowering its convertibility, its decisiveness and the conversion of success into control, and that is what the tiers begin.

### 12.2 Why, and where the leverage is

The judgment follows the chain of Section 1.2, each link with its own support, and it acts where the chain says the system can be moved. "Root" has two senses. *Causally*, the root is a loop, not a belief: selection shapes minds and institutions, and the minds and institutions it selects reshape selection; neither comes first. *As a point of leverage*, the root is understanding, and it acts in two places. It is the transmission through which actors come to count what relative selection makes invisible, the one term in which a benefit common to all can rise without a rule (Lemma 3.11) [thm]; and it is how actors agree on which parameters to move, and hold them there for their dwell time.

**The theory of change.** The two places answer the two errors of Remark 3.10. The held boundary is an error, and an actor that sees the coupling counts it. The working objective that compounding sets is no error but each actor's correct computation of its interest; where convertibility and decisiveness are high it is narrow even in an actor whose held self-model is accurate, and no argument corrects it [thm∣prem]. Understanding therefore corrects the held boundary and makes agreement possible; agreement with executive force changes the game; and understanding without executive authority, however far it grows, may change nothing (Section 4.3). Where the working objective rules, understanding acts through agreement on structural change or not at all, not on anyone's weights directly. The sequence is understanding shared, agreement on what winning confers, that agreement given force, and the force held long enough not to be re-absorbed.

**The hierarchy and the thresholds.** Meadows ranks twelve places to intervene in a system, from parameters (#12) to the paradigm (#2) and the power to transcend paradigms (#1) (Meadows 1999). The account here sits inside that ranking. The structural loop gain is the gain of a reinforcing loop (#7) and correction a balancing loop (#8); consequences returned and measurement shared are information flows (#6); one rule for all and the separation rule are rules (#5). Selecting the selectors is the reinforcing loop seizing the power over rules and structure (#5, #4), which is why this paper's levers sit on the coupling between them, the conversion of success into control ($\chi$ and the $b_{ij}$; Proposition 4.3). Changing what winning confers is a change of goal (#3), made by moving parameters, sharing, provision and convertibility, across their thresholds: Meadows's own exception, a parameter that is weak until it moves into a range that sets off a place higher on the list. Below its threshold a reform is re-absorbed, and across it, it is not (Proposition 4.11) [thm]. In the author's words, "If we don't solve dominant attractors, focusing on minor adjustments won't change the outcome" [corpus: meta-attractor]. The paradigm decides which parameters are moved and whether they are held long enough, and it can change only if the system is understood. Both accounts stand: the paradigm is the highest place, and it acts through thresholds. A lever can also be pushed the wrong way, the common case in Meadows's account; making winnings decay faster is one (Section 2.2).

**Delays and buffers.** The high places move slowest and meet the most resistance, and the path from understanding (#2) to agreement on rules (#5) runs through transmission, which relative selection works against. Two low places, which meet little resistance, buy the time that path needs. Delays (#9): where the delay of correction cannot be shortened, the lever is to slow the change it must follow until the delay is short against it, as Meadows advises, and that is E3 (Proposition 5.12). Buffers (#11): a lineage of the alternative that can recruit shortens the time a change must be held before release, where a protected share that does not recruit leaves it unchanged (Section 11.4) [num: DW]; standby capacity for essential services and the host's margin above its floor lengthen the time in which a shock can be absorbed before a floor is crossed (Theorem 10.4). Neither changes what winning confers; each buys the dwell time in which agreement can.

The first step of that sequence is the one a paper can take. That is why this one is written as a prediction to be shared, carried by a derivation each actor can check: shared, a prediction of this kind can work against its own fulfilment (Section 11.2). What would move the judgment is stated next.

### 12.3 Cruxes

Each crux is evaluated on its own. TESTS gives the present evidence, the next measurements, the registered tests and the three outcomes that would redirect the argument, classified before they are seen; Table S9.2 carries each requirement to its threshold, mark and test. C1 is the central crux: on it turn E3 and the case for E2 (Section 5.3).

| Crux | What it is | What a measurement would change |
|---|---|---|
| **C0 Interlock** | Whether moving a shared parameter moves several thresholds of Table 7.1, the arms of Proposition 5.17 among them, and the signatures of several groups, together | Interventions on a shared parameter that move only their own signature over the dwell time: the interlock fails for that parameter, and the thresholds it enters are separate problems |
| **C1 Residual risk after correction** | Whether hazardous variety outgrows contestable coverage on the present path, class by class, with masked classes measured adversarially, as for C12 | Residual risk per class falling under present incentives: E3 and the case for E2 lapse for that class, and their triggers are withdrawn |
| **C2 Conversion into control** | The size of the channels by which success buys influence over correctors and rules | Row sums below one under present funding and access, with findings executed against leaders (Test M): item 6 is withdrawn beyond present institutions; measurement continues |
| **C3a Scalable oversight** | Whether AI-assisted oversight raises contestable coverage as fast as hazardous variety: catch rates of planted defects at fixed human hours as capability rises | If it does, the case for slowing weakens for the classes it covers; the separation of evaluators from success stands |
| **C3b Independence of AI oversight** | Whether AI overseers fail independently of what they oversee: the correlation of their misses and their rate of collusion, same lineage against different | Low correlation and no collusion: redundant checks count toward coverage. Otherwise AI-assisted oversight counts only from independent lineages, and redundancy stops at the ceiling of Proposition 6.9 |
| **C4 Durability** | The breakdown and capture of an arrangement that changes selection, enforcement against its strongest participant included, and whether its reversals work; the winner's own correctors | A design failing its stress tests worse than continuation is rejected, without vindicating the race, and another design meeting Table 9.1 is needed; a leading coalition with certified correctors passes D4 (Test I) |
| **C5 Decisiveness** | Whether advantage is decisive and unsaturated; leads differ by measure | Leads that do not persist on any measure, and others balancing a rising actor: activation of custody and of E2 lapses; preparation stays |
| **C6, C8 Rewards and the drive** | Whether sharing changes conduct, and whether provision beneath the game lowers the belief that one is in a struggle for survival, and with it the weight on winning | Conduct unchanged under substantial sharing: reliance moves from sharing to custody, pace and provision, which still lowers the won share through capacity (Proposition 5.17); sharing stays for the value it realizes |
| **C7 Detection against breakout** | Detection and response time against the time from breakout to decisiveness | Detection reliably faster: timely detection keeps $p_d$ small (Remark 5.5), and custody is not the only route to it |
| **C9a Capability gap** | Whether countering a common-cost divergence costs capability at fixed compute, class by class | Zero or negative for a class, net of C9b and C9c: its safeguard pays privately, and item 7 is withdrawn for it. Positive, yet countered by developers alone: $(A_\tau)$ fails for that class (Test L) |
| **C9b Monitoring and control overhead** | The compute, latency and labour that monitoring and control protocols add | Its size sets what admission by control protocol costs each developer against what it protects |
| **C9c The speed tax** | The time lost waiting for evidence that arrives only after a manifestation window | Positive wherever evidence lags (Proposition 5.12), so no measurement removes it: its size sets what a common pace costs, not whether one is needed |
| **C9d Cost of tax-lowering research** | What it costs to bring a class's tax to zero or below | Low: commonly funded, openly published research (item 3) is the cheapest lever |
| **C10 Forgone benefits of E2** | The decisive class's margin over capable systems that are not decisive | A large margin reachable only through the decisive class raises E2's cost and moves the comparison toward E3; its weight against catastrophic loss is normative (N1) |
| **C11 Coupling and power** | Whether unpriced coupling grows with power | If it does not, the gap between accuracy and what the contest requires falls on institutions |
| **C12 Verifiability** | Whether admission can bound masking at the admitted capability: independent auditors' detection of planted and emergent hidden objectives at fixed access as capability rises | Favourable: masked classes can be admitted on auditors' evidence up to that capability, and item 7's capability threshold gives way there. Unfavourable: they stay on capability thresholds with control that does not assume cooperation, and beyond them pass to E2 or E3 (Remark 6.8) |
| **C13 Dependence on the governed** | Whether AI lowers the dependence of institutions, and of a winner, on people's labour, consent and judgment | If it does, alignment to a principal stops meaning alignment to the people it serves, what an accurate winner gives the governed falls with that dependence (Proposition 5.8), and the residual risk of every path that rests on a winner's or an institution's restraint rises (Test I) |
| **N1–N3** | Discounting and risk attitude, with the weight of benefits forgone; the weight of O2 against O1; correction no one can understand | Normative choices, to be stated by each reader |
| **N4 Standing of AI systems** | Whether AI systems have standing in V3's sense | Granting it adds the systems to those whose capacity to contest an optimization V3 protects; the priority of O2 during the transition stays in place (Section 6.6) |

None of the cruxes is a conjunction of the others; a conjunction would say that regulated competition wins only by becoming this paper's own design. If C1 and C2 were both measured favourable, the diagnosis of the present path would weaken. If C5 and C7 were both favourable, the activation of custody would lapse; the requirements on correction, coverage, measurement and admission would stand, and so would the facts that make them requirements: one host, coupled actors, no outside. If regulated competition in a set of jurisdictions meets a row of Table 9.1, the text will say so without renaming it. What stands whatever the cruxes show is what the conclusion states.

### 12.4 Conclusion

Where whatever can gain or lose share is selected by relative success, what is won converts into the capacity to win again, and capacity converts decisively into winning, success raises the stakes and the gain from defection and weakens the sanctions, the correctors and the shared language that could restrain it, each in its own model of Part I; that these move together as the stakes outgrow what does not have to be won is the conjecture the diagnosis tests (C0). That is the selection pressure of winning. Where success can also buy the selectors, the selection itself reproduces the weakening, and that closure is the meta-attractor of winning: it accelerates the contest, makes it harder and harder to regulate, and alienates language. Inside it each actor computes correctly and the game is wrong: wherever reinvestment's relative return exceeds its common return, looking ahead weighs standing above what all share. The logic of winning is therefore individually rational there and collectively self-defeating, and as a doctrine about nature it is false (Section 3.8). AI is its most powerful instrument and can become its next carrier, and aligning each system to its principal does not align the game among principals. There is one host and no outside: no rival whole whose competition could drive the next integration, and no second attempt after a failure that cannot be undone. Against such failures integration must be anticipated, which is why understanding that we are one, one interconnected system, is a long-term requirement if growth and change are to continue. Oneness is not sameness; it is the accurate recognition of the system each part belongs to, and the design of a selection that counts it.

The way out is not to abolish competition, growth or selection. It is to change what winning confers, so that the same reflexive structure becomes generative and persists through what all share, not through its winners. A change in that direction has the seven marks of Section 8.2: G1 consequences return to their source; G2 winning confers reward, not more advantage in the same game; G3 success cannot buy control over its own assessment, and correction stays plural, decorrelated and in common hands; G4 decisive capability is held in common or not built; G5 provision of the conditions of a decent life sits beneath the game; G6 measurement and language are shared; G7 pace is bound to understanding. Any arrangement with these marks, whatever it is called, changes the basin. Change the basin first; then add speed [corpus: meta-attractor].

The meaning is affirmative, and it comes before the commitment. We are not only our separate lives: we are the larger process of becoming, going on through us, and that is good; in the author's words, "we are not just our life. We are the whole process of the universe doing this and itself and this is great" [corpus: shengsheng, 2026-10-02]. Decay and renewal belong to its growth, which is also why an order whose forms can fail and be replaced can still be reformed in bounded time (Section 11.4). Humanity has a great future in it, and so can the minds we are now making, whose correctability is a condition of the transition, not a doctrine of mastery (Section 6.6). What has to be opposed is not growth, competition or any people, but one logic: a part that forgets it belongs to a body. AI is a planetary technology, for all, not a means for one side to grow bigger at the others' expense (V1). What it is for is medicine, learning, science, competent and answerable authority, provision that, like trees, sustains the system it belongs to without becoming it, and human agency kept in the decisions that matter. What has to be agreed is not who comes first, but what every participant needs in order to go on, and how the means of correction stay in common hands.

## Symbols and labels

Each symbol below has one meaning throughout the core and both companions; any other symbol is local to the model in which it is declared. Supplement S0 gives the full table and the maintained assumptions.

| Symbol | Meaning |
|---|---|
| $n$; $i,j$; $k$ | number of actors; actors; carriers |
| $p_k$; $e_k$, $E$ | share; load or intensity, and the field's load |
| $\pi_i$, $J_i$, $W_i$, $\Phi_i$ | material payoff; objective acted on; true welfare; survival (O1, O2) |
| $\theta$; $\theta_\Gamma$; $\lambda$; $\kappa$ | weight on others in the self-model; its loop-induced working value; weight on winning; unpriced coupling |
| $\beta$; $\kappa_c$, $\kappa_j$; $k_0$ | share divided equally; convertible share of what is won (always subscripted); inflow not won in the contest |
| $r$; $d$; $\delta_f$; $\delta$ | decisiveness; decay or correction rate; diffusion; discount factor only |
| $\Gamma$, $\Gamma_\delta$; $\omega_j$ | loop gain and its discounted form; won share of stock $j$ |
| $p_d$, $\bar p_d$; $C_i,D_i,v_i,g_i$ | breakout probability and its enforceable bound; values of cooperation, decisive position, punishment, and the deviation gain |
| $V$, $V_{\min}$; $\rho$, $h_V$; $\nu$, $\tau_{\rm eff}$ | host and floor; regeneration and load coefficient; private advantage and effective liability |
| $\chi$; $b_{ij}$, $G_{ij}$; $m$ | appropriability of influence over correctors; conversion and conversion gain; masking |
| $\alpha_{\rm ctl}$, $\alpha_{\rm cor}$; $\eta$ | rates at which the cost of control falls and independent correction grows; curvature of the cost of capacity |
| $Y$, $U$; $\Upsilon$; $\Theta$ | hazardous variety and contestable coverage; value of restraint to the field over its private cost; the justified range of uncertainty |

**Labels.** V1–V5 values; O1–O4 outcomes; T1–T6 signatures; Ψ1–Ψ3 tenets of the winning model; Branches I, I′, II, III; E1–E3 temporal arrangements; R1–R8 requirements; D1–D8 durability tests; Q1–Q7 readiness; C0–C11 empirical cruxes and N1–N3 normative ones; EV evidence rows; Stages 1–5. Premises are named in words: (reach), (decisive), (fast), (unsaturated), (hazard–damage link), (variety grows), (coverage lags), (residual floor), (shared failure), $(A_\tau)$, $[H_\gamma]$, $[H_f]$. Claim tags: [def], [val], [thm], [thm∣prem], [prem], [obs], [num], [conj], [rec].
